9-3-10: Nonliability Of Town
9-3-11: Changes In Fees
9-3-1: DEFINITIONS:
   As used in this chapter, the terms "water system", "waterworks", and "water
distribution system" shall mean the waterworks utility system of the town.
(1994 Code § 17-201)
9-3-2: CONNECTION TO SYSTEM; AUTHORITY OF TOWN:
   The town shall make, inspect and maintain all connections to its water lines
upon the payment to the town by the user or beneficiary thereof of the charge
prescribed by section
2-2-7 of this code. It shall be unlawful for any person, other than a duly
authorized agent or employee of the town, to make a connection to any water
line belonging to or controlled by the town. (1994 Code § 17-203; amd. 2004
Code)
9-3-3: METERS:
   A.   Metered Water Required: Water from the waterworks system shall be
furnished to customers only through meters.
   B.   Installation Of Meters: All meters shall be set under the directions of
the public works superintendent. (1994 Code § 17-202; amd. 2004 Code)
   C.   Register Kept: The public works superintendent shall maintain a
register of all meter locations, which shall show the position of each and
every meter on the property where it is located, the meter size, model and the
date of its installation. The register will further show by whom the meter was
installed, and for whom and whether or not the meter was first tested and in
good serviceable condition before installation for use. (1994 Code § 17-204;
amd. 2004 Code)
9-3-4: RESPONSIBILITY OF CONSUMER FOR SERVICE LINE:
   Each consumer of water furnished by the town shall lay his own pipeline from
the premises to the property line for the purpose of conveying water to such
point on his premises as he may desire and shall thereafter be required to keep
the same in good repair and sanitary condition. (1994 Code § 17-205)
9-3-5: WATER RATES:
   The rates to be charged to users of water furnished by the Wayne public
works authority for the town shall be as provided in section
2-2-7 of this code. (Ord. 127, 11-4-1996; amd. 2004 Code)
9-3-6: INTERRUPTION OF SERVICE:
   The town reserves the right to turn off the water in its mains at any time
for the purpose of making repairs or in the event of a breakdown in equipment
or machinery. (1994 Code § 17-207)
9-3-7: EMERGENCY WATER SHORTAGES:
   A.   Authority Of Mayor: Whenever an emergency exists by reason of a
shortage of water due to inadequate supply, limited treatment or distribution
capacity or failure of equipment or material, the mayor is hereby authorized to
restrict or prohibit the use of water from the town's water system. (1994 Code
§ 17-221)
   B.   Determination Of Emergency: An emergency exists whenever the mayor
reasonably determines that the town's water system is unable to or will, within
sixty (60) days, become unable to supply the full commercial and domestic needs
of the users thereof, including adequate fire protection. (1994 Code § 17-222)
   C.   Proclamation Of Emergency:
      1.   Issuance Of Proclamation:
         a.   Upon the determination that such an emergency exists, the mayor
shall issue a proclamation declaring the emergency and setting out with
particularity an order restricting use of water from the town system. Such
order may:
            (1)   Restrict water usage during certain periods of the day or
week or according to any orderly and nondiscriminatory scheme; and
            (2)   Prohibit usages not essential to public health and safety.
         b.   The order may be revised from time to time as the mayor deems
necessary. (1994 Code § 17-223)
      2.   Notice Of Proclamation:
         a.   The proclamation required by subsection C1 of this section shall
be published in a newspaper of general circulation in the town, or if there is
no such newspaper in which the proclamation may be published, within twenty
four (24) hours after the emergency arises, publication shall be by posting a
copy of the proclamation in ten (10) prominent places in the town. The
emergency proclamation shall be in full force and effect upon publication.
Substantial compliance with this subsection C2 is sufficient to effect the
proclamation.
         b.   Whenever a sudden or unexpected event so reduces the availability
of water or water pressure as to create an immediate threat to public health or
safety, the notice of the proclamation may be given by any reasonable means,
including electronic means. The emergency shall be in full force and effect
upon such notice. If any such means is other than that required in subsection
C2a of this section, the proclamation shall be republished in accordance with
said subsection C2a within twenty four (24) hours of the first notice. (1994
Code § 17-224)
      3.   Effect Of Proclamation: A duly proclaimed emergency shall continue
and the terms of the proclamation shall be in full force for thirty (30) days
or until such time as the mayor or the town board of trustees shall cause to be
published a proclamation that the emergency has ended, whichever is shorter,
unless the town board of trustees, by resolution approved by a majority of all
its members, extends the proclamation. (1994 Code § 17-225)
      4.   Appeals: Any person feeling aggrieved by a proclamation of the mayor
shall have the right to present the matter to the next regular or special
meeting of the town board of trustees or to any emergency session called to
discuss the water emergency. The town board of trustees may exempt such
aggrieved person, wholly or in part, from compliance with the proclamation
order upon a showing that compliance creates an immediate threat to the
person's health or safety. The ruling of the town board of trustees by a
majority vote of all its members shall be final and binding as to the
continuance of any terms of the proclamation. Until and unless the action of
the mayor is modified or revoked by action of the town board of trustees, all
water users shall be bound by the proclamation. (1994 Code § 17-226)
   D.   Violation; Penalty: Any person who in any manner directly or indirectly
violates or permits others under his supervision, custody or control to violate
any term of a duly published proclamation shall be guilty of a misdemeanor.
Each separate day of water use in violation of such proclamation shall
constitute a separate offense. Violations of this section shall be punishable
as provided in section
1-4-1 of this code. (1994 Code § 17-227)
9-3-8: TAMPERING WITH OR INJURY TO SYSTEM 1 :
   It shall be unlawful for any person to injure or deface or in any way tamper
with any hydrant, stopcock, corporation cock, meter, valve, pipeline, or any
part of the waterworks system, or to turn the water off or on from any main at
any time or place, unless he is duly authorized so to do by the town board.
(1994 Code § 17-208)
 
Notes
1 See also section
  6-5A-5 of this code.
9-3-9: ENTRY POWERS:
   Consumers taking water from the town shall, at all reasonable hours, grant
any person authorized by the town board free access to all parts of the
premises to which water is delivered for the purpose of inspection and
examination of fixtures or to read the meters. (1994 Code § 17-206)
9-3-10: NONLIABILITY OF TOWN:
   All persons taking water from the mains shall do so at their own risk, and
the town shall not be held responsible for any accident or damage of any kind
or character occasioned by pressure in the water mains, the shutting off of the
water, or for failure to keep water under pressure in the mains for any reason.
(1994 Code § 17-207)
9-3-11: CHANGES IN FEES:
   The water tap and water service charge set forth in section
2-2-7 of this code, may be increased or decreased by motion or resolution of
the town board of trustees. (Ord. 2005-001, 2-7-2005)
***** CHAPTER 4
SEWER USE AND SERVICE *****
SECTION:
9-4-1: Authority Of Town
9-4-2: Use Of The Public System Required
9-4-3: Sanitary Sewer Tap Guidelines
9-4-4: Sewer User Fees
9-4-1: AUTHORITY OF TOWN:
   The Town shall allow connections to the Town’s sewer lines within the Town
limits upon payment to the Town by the user or beneficiary thereof. (Ord. 2021-
TITLE9, 12-6-2021, eff. 1-5-2022)
9-4-2: USE OF THE PUBLIC SYSTEM REQUIRED:
   Each occupied residence and each occupied business or commercial building in
the town shall connect to the town's sanitary sewer system within sixty (60)
days after the effective date hereof; provided, however, that such service
shall be brought within at least one hundred fifty feet (150') of the residence
or business property line. Any person, firm, corporation or entity violating
any provision of this section shall, upon conviction thereof, be liable to a
fine not to exceed fifty dollars ($50.00), and each day that any violation of
any provision of this section is allowed to exist shall be and constitute a
separate offense. (Ord. 123, 3-14-1996; amd. 2004 Code)
9-4-3: SANITARY SEWER TAP GUIDELINES:
   A.   No unmetered water, nor any surface drainage will be allowed to enter
the sanitary sewer system. The approval plan application and the issuance of a
permit does not legalize nor authorize any work which is not in compliance with
the Code of the Town of Wayne or Wayne Public Works Authority Sanitary
regulations.
   B.   1.   The sewer tap must be made by a licensed plumber and inspected by
WPWA.
      2.   The homeowner is responsible for digging and uncovering the main
line and covering up.
      3.   The padding on the pipe shall be of class I material on all lines.
      4.   The tap shall be no closer than three feet (3') from a manhole.
      5.   Tap must be at a forty-five (45) degree angle on the main.
      6.    If the tap is made over the weekend, the ditch will be left open or
will be dug up at the expense of the homeowner to be inspected.
      7.   Trench excavation must meet Federal Safety Requirements.
      8.   Any sanitary sewer connection into the Wayne Public Works Authority
collection system made outside WPWA limits shall require the approval of the
WPWA Board.
   C.   Discharge Into Public Sewer Or Natural Outlets Prohibited: No septic
tank or cesspool shall be permitted to discharge to any public sewer or natural
outlet. (Ord. 2021-TITLE9, 12-6-2021, eff. 1-5-2022)
9-4-4: SEWER USER FEES:
   A.   Tap Fees: The charge for making a sewer tap and connection shall be as
provided in section
2-2-7 of this code.
   B.   Service Fees:
      1.   Fees Established: For each dwelling, business or other establishment
of any kind connected to the town's sewer system, there shall be assessed a
sewer service fee based on the monthly water consumption, being at those rates
set out in section
2-2-7 of this code.
      2.   Payment Of Fees; Delinquencies: The sewer service charge shall be
collected by and paid to the town clerk-treasurer, and collection procedure
shall be the same as for water service 1
. Service may be discontinued for failure to pay said sewer charge, even though
charges for water have been paid and are not in arrears in respect to the
premises for which the sewer service charge is delinquent.
   C.   Changes In Fees: The sewer tap fee and sewer service charge set forth
in subsections A and B of this section may be increased or decreased by motion
or resolution of the town board of trustees. (Ord. 119, 4-3-1995; amd. 2004
Code)
 
Notes
1 1 See section
  9-2-6  of this title.
***** CHAPTER 5
SOLID WASTE AND TRASH DISPOSAL AND COLLECTION *****
SECTION:
9-5-1: Purpose
9-5-2: Definitions
9-5-3: Collection And Disposal A Municipal Function
9-5-4: Accumulation Of Solid Waste Prohibited; Nuisance Declared
9-5-5: Receptacles
9-5-6: Bulk And Yard Waste
9-5-7: Waste From Building Construction
9-5-8: Collection And Disposal Of Solid Waste
9-5-9: Bills For Service; Delinquencies
9-5-10: Burning Waste
9-5-11: Grinding Waste
9-5-12: Disposal Of Animal Carcasses
9-5-1: PURPOSE:
   It is the purpose of this chapter and it is hereby declared to be the policy
of the town, pursuant to the authority of the Oklahoma solid waste management
act (27A Oklahoma Statutes sections 2-10-101–2-10-1001, inclusive), to regulate
the collection and disposal of solid wastes in a manner that will protect the
public health and welfare, prevent air and water pollution, prevent the spread
of disease and the creation of nuisances, conserve the natural resources, and
enhance and preserve the beauty and quality of the town's environment. (1994
Code § 17-402; amd. 2004 Code)
9-5-2: DEFINITIONS:
As used in this chapter, the following words and terms shall have the meanings
respectively ascribed to them in this section:
PERSON: Any individual, corporation, company, firm partnership, association,
trust or other legal entity, however constituted or organized.
REFUSE: Tree trimmings, junked building materials and roofing materials,
manufacturing waste, rocks, dirt and other waste material not defined in this
section as "solid waste" or "trash".
SOLID WASTE: All putrescible and nonputrescible refuse in solid or semisolid
form, including, but not limited to, garbage, refuse, rubbish, ashes or
incinerator residue, street refuse, dead animals, demolition wastes,
construction wastes, solid or semisolid commercial and industrial wastes and
hazardous wastes, including explosives, pathogenic wastes, chemical wastes,
herbicide and pesticide wastes.
SOLID WASTE MANAGEMENT SYSTEM: The entire process of storage, collection,
transportation, processing and burying of solid wastes at or in a site approved
by the Oklahoma state department of health.
TRASH: Paper, rags, containers of paper, tin cans, yard and house sweepings and
all other household waste; but not tree trimmings, building and manufacturing
waste, sewage and rocks, raw dirt, rainwater and other liquid refuse properly
disposable through the sanitary sewers of the town.
(1994 Code § 17-403)
9-5-3: COLLECTION AND DISPOSAL A MUNICIPAL FUNCTION 1 :
   The collection and disposal of garbage, trash and refuse is hereby declared
to be a municipal function of the town as a protection of the public health;
the police powers of the town shall be invoked when necessary for the
enforcement of this chapter. In addition, the town may collect and dispose of
refuse as it deems necessary; provided, that such disposal shall be by an
approved method of incineration (not open burning) or by landfill and daily
cover. (1994 Code § 17-401)
 
Notes
1    See section
  2-2-7 of this code for rates and charges.
9-5-4: ACCUMULATION OF SOLID WASTE PROHIBITED; NUISANCE DECLARED 2 :
   It is unlawful for any person in charge of any lot or piece of ground to
allow solid waste to accumulate thereon so as to cause an offensive odor to be
emitted therefrom or to become injurious or dangerous to the health of the
neighborhood or any inhabitant thereof. Any such condition is hereby declared a
violation of this chapter and punishable as such and, in addition, is declared
to be a nuisance and may be abated as such. (1994 Code § 17-404)
 
Notes
2    See also subsections
  5-2-3A12 and A18b of this code.
9-5-5: RECEPTACLES:
   A.   Use Required: It is unlawful for any person to deposit for collection
or other purpose any solid waste on any street, alley, or public parking, or on
any private property, unless the same is deposited in cans or containers as
herein provided.
   B.   Specifications: It is the duty of all persons in charge of any premises
or piece of ground in the town to place all solid waste in watertight metallic
or plastic cans or containers of sufficient size to hold not less than five (5)
nor more than thirty (30) gallons, and have close fitting covers and handles on
the sides.
   C.   Placement For Pick Up; Bulk Receptacles:
      1.   If the town or its contract solid waste disposal agent furnishes or
otherwise provides bulk metal receptacles or containers on the public rights of
way for use by persons residing or doing business on a block for which the
receptacles or containers are furnished or provided, persons in charge of
premises, lots or pieces of ground within the town shall transfer the solid
waste accumulated on their own premises and contained in receptacles or
containers, as herein prescribed, to the block containers furnished or provided
by the town or its solid waste disposal contract agent.
      2.   If the town or its contract disposal agent does not furnish
receptacles on the public right of way for the collection of solid wastes on a
block basis, then the cans or containers herein required shall be placed by the
person in charge of the premises or piece of ground on the alley or at some
convenient place on the lot, premises or piece of ground so as to provide
access thereto by a collector.
   D.   Maintenance Of Receptacles: Containers used for holding combustible or
noncombustible wastes shall be maintained in good condition. Any waste
container that does not conform to the provisions of this chapter or that may
have ragged or sharp edges or any other defect liable to hamper or injure the
person collecting the contents thereof must be promptly replaced by a proper
receptacle upon receipt of notice to that effect from the collector. If not so
replaced within ten (10) days after receipt of such notice, the nonconforming
or defective container may be collected and disposed of by the collector. (1994
Code § 17-404)
9-5-6: BULK AND YARD WASTE:
   All bulk waste accumulated in residential, commercial, or manufacturing
buildings, such as cardboard containers, wooden crates, hedge trimmings, and
similar refuse, shall be flattened and tied in bundles or packed in suitable
containers, not more than twenty (20) gallons in size, that may be readily
handled by one collector. In no case shall such bundle be larger than two feet
by three feet by four feet (2' x 3' x 4'). Refuse shall be tied into bundles
which can be readily handled by one collector. Tree limbs exceeding eight
inches (8") in diameter will not be collected by the town and should not be
included in any bundle for refuse collection. (1994 Code § 17-404)
9-5-7: WASTE FROM BUILDING CONSTRUCTION:
   Any person constructing any structure within the town shall, prior to
construction on any premises in the town, furnish and place on the premises a
container of suitable size and design to contain all solid waste which might,
by the winds or elements, be distributed and blown from the premises. The
container shall be used by the person constructing at all times to keep the
premises from becoming unsightly with solid waste. Any person who fails to keep
the premises on which any structure is being built or installed free from solid
waste and which allows the waste to blow or be carried from the premises to
adjoining or other property or into the town streets shall be deemed guilty of
causing a nuisance and shall be subject to the penalties in this code 1  or
ordinances prescribed therefor. (1994 Code § 17-404)
 
Notes
1    See title 5, chapter 2 of this code for nuisance regulations.
9-5-8: COLLECTION AND DISPOSAL OF SOLID WASTE:
   A.   Exclusive Authority Of Contractor: It shall be unlawful for any person,
except under contract as is herein provided, to remove or cause to be removed
any solid waste materials from any garbage can or other receptacle used for the
deposit of same, or to transport or haul, carry or otherwise move over any of
the streets, alleys or other public ways any solid waste materials. (1994 Code
§ 17-406)
   B.   Frequency Of Collection: Collections of garbage and trash, and the
removal thereof to some suitable place outside the town limits, shall be made
at least once each week. (1994 Code § 17-409)
9-5-9: BILLS FOR SERVICE; DELINQUENCIES:
   A.   Charges Included With Water And Sewer Bill: To facilitate the
collection of the rates and charges herein levied and assessed, the clerk-
treasurer or other designated agent of the town is hereby further directed to
include the charges on the same bill by which the town bills for and collects
for its sewer, water or other services, to the end that the rates and charges
herein levied shall be paid and collected by the town at the same time 1 . The
rates and charges above levied shall be shown on the bill as a separate and
distinct item of charge.
   B.   Delinquent Payments 2 : In the event any owner, occupant or person in
charge of any user unit against whom a charge has been herein levied and
assessed fails or otherwise refuses to pay the charge, the provisions of
section
9-2-6 of this title shall be applicable. (1994 Code § 17-410; amd. 2004 Code)
 
Notes
1    See section
  9-2-6 of this title.
2    See also section
  9-2-6 of this title.
9-5-10: BURNING WASTE:
   A.   Prohibitions:
      1.   It shall be unlawful for any person to start or maintain any fire or
cause any fire to be started or maintained for the purpose of burning or
incinerating any solid waste, including but not limited to, grass, stubble,
agricultural/garden waste, vegetation, tree limbs or clippings that may be on
any property within the corporate limits of Wayne without first paying for and
receiving from the Town of Wayne, or a designee thereof, including the Fire
Chief, a permit authorizing the same. The cost of such permit shall be ten
dollars ($10.00).
         a.   Burn permits shall be issued for a specific date and time and
pre-approved by the Fire Chief or fire department designee. The permit shall
only be approved immediately prior to the time of the actual bum.
         b.   Burn permits may be issued only under the following weather
conditions, and any other added conditions that may from time to time be deemed
necessary by the Fire Chief or fire department designee for the protection of
the public and property:
         c.   No plastic, tar, rubber, chemical, hazardous material, wiring,
shingles, treated or painted building materials, or synthetic fibrous material
shall be burned. Only biodegradable materials such as wood, leaves, grass
(clippings), hay, etc., may be burned. lt is the responsibility of the
landowner and/or resident to inform the Fire Chief or fire department designee
of all materials to be burned.
         d.   All bums within the limits of Wayne shall be attended at all
times.
         e.   Bum permits may be issued beginning at seven thirty (7:30) a.m.
Initial ignition of any controlled bum, shall not take place after five o’clock
(5:00) p.m. All burns shall be extinguished by sunset.
      2.   It shall be unlawful to construct or install, within any building in
the Town of Wayne, any incinerator designed to burn solid wastes.
      3.   It shall be unlawful to ignite or cause to be ignited any fire
within the town limits of the Town of Wayne, except as specifically set forth
below.
         a.   Fires within a properly constructed fireplace or heating stove
used for the purpose of providing heat within the confines of a structure;
         b.   Any fire built in an outside receptacle, properly contained, for
the purpose of outdoor cooking, camping or bon fire;
         c.   The burning of paper or cardboard wa te products within a
properly constructed, enclosed incinerator operating at any commercial or
retail establishment within the town limits.
      4.   Bum permits shall not be issued during a Town, county or state bum
ban. A landowner and/or resident of Wayne shall comply with Town, county or
state burn bans if said landowner and/or resident's property is located within
the area affected by the ban.
      5.   A landowner and/or resident who receives a bum permit and
negligently, carelessly, or intentionally allows a fire to spread beyond the
bounds of its intended confinement shall be subject to a maximum fine of two
hundred dollars ($200.00) per occurrence plus costs. Additional costs, to be
determined by the Wayne Board of Trustees may be imposed if fire department
equipment and personnel are required to respond for extinguishment purposes.
(1994 Code § 17-405; amd. 2004 Code; Ord. 2025-05, 12-13-2025)
9-5-11: GRINDING WASTE:
   It shall be unlawful to construct or to install within any retail store or
other commercial or industrial establishment within the town any mechanical or
power driven device or machine for the grinding of solid waste materials
designed to be introduced into the sewer system of the town, unless the same
has been approved by the public works superintendent or other designated
appointee of the town, to ensure that such waste will not block or otherwise
interfere with the sewer lines. (1994 Code § 17-407; amd. 2004 Code)
9-5-12: DISPOSAL OF ANIMAL CARCASSES:
   Carcasses of animals must be transported to the town sanitary landfill by or
at the expense of the owner of the animal. It shall be unlawful for any person
to bury or cause to be buried the carcass of any animal at any place within the
town. (1994 Code § 17-408)
TITLE 10
BUILDING REGULATIONS
***** CHAPTER 1
BUILDING CODE AND REGULATIONS *****
SECTION:
10-1-1: Building Code
10-1-2: Building Permits
10-1-3: Fire Limits
10-1-4: Address Numbering
10-1-1: BUILDING CODE:
   A.   The International Building Code, International Existing Building Code,
International Residential Code, and International Property Maintenance Code, as
published and adopted by the International Code Council, shall hereby be
adopted by reference, with the latest version published by the International
Code Council being maintained by the Town Clerk and adopted for purposes of
regulation, governance, and enforcement of the Building Code of the Town of
Wayne, in the State of Oklahoma.
   B.   That three (3) true and correct copies of all related Building Codes
shall at all times be kept and maintained on file in the office of the Town
Clerk of the Town of Wayne, Oklahoma, marked and designated by title. (1994
Code §§ 5-101 - 5-103; amd. 2004 Code; Ord. 2025-04, 2-13-2024)
10-1-2: BUILDING PERMITS:
   A.   Permit Required: It is unlawful for any person, firm, or corporation to
construct, alter, or move a building or structure, or to begin to do the same,
without securing from the town clerk-treasurer a permit therefor.
   B.   Fee: For a permit for the construction or alteration of a building or
structure, the fee shall be as set by the board of trustees by motion or
resolution 1 . (1994 Code § 5-105)
 
Notes
1    See section
  2-2-8 of this code.
10-1-3: FIRE LIMITS:
   The boundaries of the fire limits of the town shall be as established by
resolution of the town board.
(1994 Code § 5-104; amd. 2004 Code)
10-1-4: ADDRESS NUMBERING:
   A.   Required: All houses and buildings fronting on public streets in the
town of Wayne shall be numbered. The owners and the occupants of all buildings
within town limits shall install and maintain the numbers of the street address
of that building upon the outside of the primary structure. These numbers shall
be displayed in a manner and have sufficient size as to be easily seen from the
public roadway by police, fire and other emergency personnel.
   B.   Specifications: The identifying numbers shall be not less than three
inches (3") in height and must be in color contrast to the house or building,
so that it can easily be seen day and night. Address numbers shall be located
either near a building's main entrance, above the garage door, or on a surface
that is visible from the street of the address. Houses or buildings that are
set back more than one hundred feet (100') from a street curb shall be required
to display numbers within fifty feet (50') of the street curb, on a surface
that is easily visible from the street of address.
   C.   Responsibility: The responsibility for the displaying of the assigned
number for each existing house and building in town limits, as of the effective
date hereof, shall rest with the property owner, trustee, lessee, agent or
occupant of each house or building.
   D.   Violation: Any person who shall fail to comply with any provision of
this section, or who shall number or attempt to number any house or building
other than in conformity with this section, or who shall fail to change his/her
number, if wrong within sixty (60) days of the effective date and thereafter
within thirty (30) days after being notified in writing to do so by the clerk,
shall be deemed guilty of a misdemeanor and subjected to a fine of not less
than twenty dollars ($20.00), nor more than one hundred dollars ($100.00) for
each offense. Each day that a violation is permitted to exist shall constitute
a separate offense.
   E.   Nuisances: In addition to any other penalties and sanctions which may
be imposed, any structure which does not have a well maintained numbering
system as required by this section is hereby declared a public nuisance, and
the town may abate such a nuisance by installing the numbers after following
the procedures set forth in this code; and the town may assess the land or
applicable utility bills with the costs of abatement as set forth in this code.
(Ord. 2015-1, 8-3-2015)
***** CHAPTER 2
PLUMBING CODE AND REGULATIONS *****
SECTION:
10-2-1: Plumbing Code
10-2-2: Contractor Registration Requirements
10-2-3: Plumbing Permits
10-2-4: Single-Family Residential Construction
10-2-5: Inspections
10-2-6: Violation; Penalty
10-2-1: PLUMBING CODE:
   A.   The International Plumbing Code, as published and adopted by the
International Code Council, is hereby adopted by reference, with the latest
version published by the International Code Council being maintained by the
Town Clerk and adopted for purposes of regulation, governance, and enforcement
of the Building Code of the Town of Wayne, in the State of Oklahoma.
   B.   That three (3) true and correct copies of all Codes adopted hereby
shall at all times be kept and maintained on file in the office of the Town
Clerk of the Town of Wayne, Oklahoma, marked and designated by title. (1994
Code §§ 5-201 - 5-202; amd. Ord. 2025-04, 2-13-2025)
10-2-2: CONTRACTOR REGISTRATION REQUIREMENTS:
   A.   Registration Required: No person shall practice or engage in the
business, trade or occupation of a plumbing contractor, a journeyman plumber,
or a plumber's apprentice, unless he is registered as such with the building
inspector of the town as required in the town plumbing code.
   B.   Fees:
      1.   Fees Established: The registration fees for plumbing contractors,
journeyman plumbers and apprentice plumbers, to be paid to the town clerk-
treasurer, are as set by the town board of trustees 1 . (1994 Code § 5-203;
amd. 2004 Code)
      2.   Payment And Disposition Of Fees: The fee shall accompany the
application, and all fees provided for in this chapter shall be paid to the
town clerk-treasurer for deposit to the account of the town.
   C.   Expiration Of Registration: All registration certificates shall expire
on December 31 of each year.
   D.   Bond And Insurance Requirements: Bond and insurance requirements of
plumbing contractors shall be satisfactory to the town and shall meet the
requirements established by state law prior to the issuance of a license or
registration. (1994 Code § 5-205; amd. 2004 Code)
 
Notes
1    See section
  2-2-8 of this code.
10-2-3: PLUMBING PERMITS:
   A.   Permit Required; Issuance To Registered Contractor: No plumbing work,
unless excepted in this code, shall be undertaken prior to the issuance of a
permit therefor by the building inspector. A permit shall be issued to a
registered plumbing contractor only, except as provided in this code. (1994
Code § 5-206; amd. 2004 Code)
   B.   Application For Permit: Application for a permit for plumbing work
shall be made on suitable forms provided by the building inspector. The
application shall be accompanied by fees in accordance with the schedule set by
the town. (1994 Code § 5-208; amd. 2004 Code)
10-2-4: SINGLE-FAMILY RESIDENTIAL CONSTRUCTION:
   Any permit required by this chapter may be issued to any person to do any
work required by this chapter in a single-family dwelling used exclusively for
living purposes, including the usual accessory buildings and quarters in
connection with such buildings, provided the person is the bona fide owner of
such dwelling, and that the same will be occupied by the owner, and that the
owner shall personally purchase all material and perform all labor in
connection therewith. The building inspector must inspect the work to see that
it complies with this chapter, and the owner shall pay the regular scheduled
fees for permits. (1994 Code § 5-207; amd. 2004 Code)
10-2-5: INSPECTIONS:
   Upon the completion of the installation of any plumbing device, or
equipment, it shall be the duty of the person, firm or corporation installing
same to notify the building inspector, and the inspector shall inspect the
installation within twenty four (24) hours after notice is given. If, upon
inspection, it is found that any part of the installation does not comply with
the provisions of this chapter, the inspector shall give notice in writing of
the violation to the person, firm or corporation to whom the permit is issued.
(1994 Code § 5-206; amd. 2004 Code)
10-2-6: VIOLATION; PENALTY:
   A violation of this chapter shall be deemed a misdemeanor and shall be
punishable as provided in section
1-4-1 of this code. Any person who violates or refuses to comply with any of
the provisions of this chapter shall be punished as provided in section
1-4-1 of this code. (1994 Code § 5-209)
***** CHAPTER 3
ELECTRICAL CODE AND REGULATIONS *****
SECTION:
10-3-1: Electrical Code Adopted
10-3-2: Contractor Registration Requirements
10-3-3: Electrical Permits
10-3-4: Single-Family Residential Construction
10-3-5: Violation; Penalty
10-3-1: ELECTRICAL CODE ADOPTED:
   The national electrical code, the latest edition thereof, as approved by the
American Standards Association, for the installation of electrical wiring,
devices and equipment is hereby adopted as the minimum standard for the
installation of all electrical wiring, devices and equipment in the town,
except as otherwise specifically provided in this chapter. The national
electrical code is hereby made a part of this code as fully and to the same
extent as if copied here in full; provided, that the present or current issue
of the national electrical code and any subsequent amendments thereto shall be
considered as the "national electrical code" as that term is used herein. In
the event of any conflict between the provisions of this chapter and the
national electrical code, then, in such event, the provisions of this chapter
shall govern. One copy of said code is on file in the office of the town clerk-
treasurer. (1994 Code § 5-301; amd. 2004 Code)
10-3-2: CONTRACTOR REGISTRATION REQUIREMENTS:
   A.   Registration Required: No person shall practice or engage in the
business, trade or occupation of an electrical contractor, journeyman
electrician or electrician's apprentice, unless he holds a current license
obtained from the state, and is registered as such with the building inspector
of the town as required in the state electrical license act 1 .
   B.   Fees: The registration fees for electrical contractors, journeyman
electricians and apprentice electricians shall be as set by the town board of
trustees 2 , and the fee shall accompany the application.
   C.   Expiration Of Registration; Renewal: All registration certificates
shall expire on December 31 of each year. Registrations are subject to renewal
on or before December 31 by paying the renewal registration fees as required by
this chapter 3 .
   D.   Bond And Insurance Requirements: Bond and insurance requirements of
electrical contractors shall be satisfactory to the town and shall meet the
requirements established by state law prior to the issuance of a license or
registration. (1994 Code § 5-304; amd. 2004 Code)
   E.   Nontransferability Of Registration: No registration issued in
accordance with the provisions of this chapter shall be transferable. Any
holder of a license who shall permit or allow same to be used by any other
party to obtain a permit to do electrical work as specified in this chapter
shall be subject to having such license revoked by the town board. (1994 Code §
5-305)
 
Notes
1 59 OS § 1850.1 et seq.
2 See section
  2-2-8 of this code.
3 See section
  2-2-8 of this code.
10-3-3: ELECTRICAL PERMITS:
   A.   Permit Required; Issuance To Registered Contractor: Before any
electrical wiring, device or equipment, subject to the provisions of this
chapter, is altered or repaired, a permit therefor must be obtained from the
building inspector. Unless the permit is for work exempted in this chapter, the
permit shall be issued only in the name of a licensed electrical contractor
upon the presentation of an application by the contractor in person. An agent
or employee of the contractor shall not be allowed to act on behalf of the
contractor. (1994 Code § 5-302; amd. 2004 Code)
   B.   Contents Of Permit: The permit shall state the location of the work to
be done, a description of the work and whether it will consist of a new
installation, or addition or repair or alteration of an old installation, and
the name of the owner or occupant of the building or premises. (1994 Code § 5-
302)
   C.   Permit Fees: Before any permit is granted for the installation,
alteration or repair of any electrical wiring, devices or equipment, the
person, firm or corporation making application for such permit shall pay to the
town a fee in the amount set by the town. All fees provided for in this chapter
shall be paid to the town clerk-treasurer for deposit to the account of the
town. (1994 Code § 5-303)
   D.   Conditions Of Permit: The permit, when issued, shall be for such
installation as described in the application, and no deviation shall be made
from the installation so described without the written approval of the building
inspector. If, upon inspection, it is found that any part of the installation
does not comply with the provisions of this chapter, the inspector shall give
notice in writing of the violation to the person, firm or corporation to whom
the permit was issued. (1994 Code § 5-302; amd. 2004 Code)
10-3-4: SINGLE-FAMILY RESIDENTIAL CONSTRUCTION:
   Any permit required by this chapter may be issued to any person to do any
work required by this chapter in a single-family dwelling used exclusively for
living purposes, including the usual accessory buildings and quarters in
connection with such buildings, provided the person is the bona fide owner of
such dwelling, and that the same will be occupied by the owner, and that the
owner shall personally purchase all material and perform all labor in
connection therewith. The building inspector must inspect the work to see that
it complies with this chapter, and the owner shall pay the regular schedule of
fees for permits. (1994 Code § 5-306; amd. 2004 Code)
10-3-5: VIOLATION; PENALTY:
   A violation of this chapter shall be deemed a misdemeanor and shall be
punishable by fine. Any person, firm or corporation who violates or refuses to
comply with any of the provisions of this chapter shall be punished as provided
in section
1-4-1 of this code. (1994 Code § 5-307)
***** CHAPTER 4
FIRE CODES AND FIRE PREVENTION REGULATIONS *****
SECTION:
10-4-1: Fire Codes
10-4-2: Permits For New Materials, Processes Or Occupancies
10-4-3: Appeals
10-4-4: Violation; Penalties
10-4-1: FIRE CODES:
   A.   The International Fire Code, as published and adopted by the
International Code Council, is hereby adopted by reference, with the latest
version published by the International Code Council being maintained by the
Town Clerk and adopted for purposes of regulation, governance, and enforcement
of the Building Code of the Town of Wayne, in the State of Oklahoma.
      1.   That three (3) true and correct copies of this Code shall at all
times be kept and maintained on file in the office of the Town Clerk of the
Town of Wayne, Oklahoma, marked and designated by title.
   B.   The NFPA 101 Life Safety Code, 2024 edition, as published by the
National as published by the National Fire Protection Association, is hereby
adopted by the Town of Wayne for regulating and governing the safety of
buildings and life; providing for the issuance of permits and collection of
fees as may be necessary therefore; and each and all of the regulations,
provisions, penalties, conditions and terms of said NFPA 101 Life Safety Code
on file in the office of the Town Clerk of the Town of Wayne are hereby
referred to, adopted, and made a part hereof, as if fully set out in this
chapter. (1994 Code §§ 13-101 and 13-102; amd. 2004 Code; Ord. 2025-03, 2-13-
2025; Ord. 2025-04, 2-13-2025)
10-4-2: PERMITS FOR NEW MATERIALS, PROCESSES OR OCCUPANCIES:
   The town board of trustees may act as a committee to determine and specify,
after giving affected persons an opportunity to be heard, any new materials,
processes, or occupancies, which shall require permits, in addition to those
now enumerated in the codes adopted herein 1 . The fire chief shall post such
list in a conspicuous place in his office and distribute copies thereof to
interested persons. (1994 Code § 13-103)
 
Notes
1    See section
  2-2-8 of this code for permit fee.
10-4-3: APPEALS:
   Whenever the chief of the fire department shall disapprove an application or
refuse to grant a permit applied for, or when it is claimed that the provisions
of the codes adopted herein do not apply or that the true intent and meaning of
said codes have been misconstrued or wrongly interpreted, the applicant may
appeal from the decision of the chief of the fire department to the town board
of trustees within thirty (30) days from the date of the decision appealed.
(1994 Code § 13-104)
10-4-4: VIOLATION; PENALTIES:
   Any person who violates any of the provisions of the codes hereby adopted or
fails to comply therewith, or who violates or fails to comply with any order
made thereunder, or who builds in violation of any detailed statement of
specifications or plans submitted and approved thereunder, or any certificate
or permit issued thereunder, and from which no appeal has been taken, or who
fails to comply with such an order as affirmed or modified by the town board of
trustees or by a court of competent jurisdiction, within the time fixed
therein, shall severally for every such violation and noncompliance
respectively, be guilty of an offense, punishable as provided in section
1-4-1 of this code. The imposition of one penalty for any violation shall not
excuse the violation or permit it to continue, and all such persons shall be
required to correct or remedy such violations or defects within a reasonable
time. When not otherwise specified, each ten (10) days that prohibited
conditions are maintained shall constitute a separate offense. The application
of this penalty shall not be held to prevent the enforced removal of prohibited
conditions. (1994 Code § 13-105)

deletions and changes, if any, prescribed in this section. State licensed
plumbing and mechanical contractors and persons authorized to install gas
piping under state licensing laws are authorized to do so under this code. One
copy of the fuel gas code is on file in the office of the town clerk-treasurer.
(1994 Code § 5-401; amd. 2004 Code)
   B.   Contractor Registration Requirements:

business, trade or occupation of a gas fitter, unless he holds a current
Oklahoma plumbing contractor's license or a current Oklahoma mechanical
contractor's license and obtains from the town clerk-treasurer a gas fitter's
registration certificate.
      2.   Fee: The fee for such registration or renewal is the same provided
for the plumbing contractor or mechanical contractor pursuant to the town code
1 . Before a permit shall be issued by the building inspector for new
installation of gas piping, repair or replacement of existing piping, or the
installation of gas fired equipment, fees as set by the town shall be paid to
the town clerk-treasurer. (1994 Code § 5-402; amd. 2004 Code)

provided the person is a bona fide owner of such dwelling, and that the same
will be occupied by the owner, and that the owner shall personally purchase all
material and perform all labor in connection therewith. The building inspector
must inspect the work to see that it complies with this section, and the owner
shall pay the regular schedule of fees for permits. (1994 Code § 5-403; amd.
2004 Code)
   D.   Penalty: Any person who shall do any act prohibited by this section or
fail or refuse to comply therewith or fail or refuse to obey a lawful order of
the building inspector shall be punished by a fine as provided in section
1-4-1 of this code. (1994 Code § 5-404; amd. 2004 Code)

***** CHAPTER 7
HOUSING CODE AND REGULATIONS *****
SECTION:
10-7-1: Reserved
10-7-2: Regulations; Nonconformities
10-7-3: Mobile Homes
10-7-1: RESERVED.
10-7-2: REGULATIONS; NONCONFORMITIES:
   A.   Definitions: For the purpose of this section, the following words shall
have the meanings ascribed to them in this subsection:
   LOT: A separate and distinct parcel of land, designated on a legally
recorded plat or a legally recorded deed filed in the records of the McClain
County clerk and containing ten (10) acres or less.
   RESIDENTIAL BUILDING: Any structure, situated on a lot, built, used, or
occupied for the residence, dwelling, or domestic shelter of any person,
including, without limitation, site built houses or structures, manufactured
housing, mobile homes, modular homes, and dwellings of all kinds. "Residential
building" does not mean jails, apartment houses, condominiums, hotels, motels,
nursing homes, or convalescent homes.
   B.   Density Requirement: No lot shall contain more than one residential
building.
   C.   Nonconforming Residential Buildings: Where residential buildings exist
on a lot at the effective date hereof that would not be permitted under the
terms of this section by reason of restrictions on the number of residential
buildings which may be placed on a lot (hereinafter called nonconforming
residential buildings), such nonconforming residential buildings and their use
as such may be continued so long as it remains otherwise lawful, subject to the
following conditions:
      1.   Alterations: No such nonconforming residential building shall be
enlarged or altered;
      2.   Moving Building: No such nonconforming residential building shall be
moved any distance for any reason;
      3.   Damage: Should a nonconforming residential building be destroyed by
any means, to the extent of more than fifty percent (50%) of its replacement
cost at the time of destruction, it shall not be reconstructed;
      4.   Discontinuance Of Use: Where the use of a nonconforming residential
building for such purpose is discontinued or abandoned for a period of six (6)
months, the nonconforming residential building shall not thereafter be used
except in conformity with this section; and
      5.   Removal Or Destruction Of Nonconforming Building; Lot Becomes
Conforming: Nonconforming status is applied to a residential building and the
lot on which it is situated, and removal or destruction of the residential
building shall eliminate the nonconforming status of the lot.
   D.   Violation; Penalty: Any person, firm or corporation who shall violate
this section shall be guilty of an offense, punishable as provided in section
1-4-1 of this code, and each day any such violation occurs or continues to
exist shall be a separate offense as provided in subsection
1-4-1B1 of this code and, upon conviction thereof, shall be punishable by fine
as provided in section
1-4-1 of this code for each such separate offense. (Ord. 130, 6-9-1997)
10-7-3: MOBILE HOMES:
   A.   Mobile homes shall not be moved into or relocated within the town.
   B.   It has become apparent that older mobile homes and manufactured homes
tend to result in blighting and rapid deterioration resulting in numerous code
violations and an overall reduction in property values. Accordingly, it is
deemed necessary to regulate such dwelling units in a manner reasonably
calculated to prevent these occurrences of such other deleterious effects upon
surrounding properties. Manufactured homes dated after January 1, 1993 shall be
allowed in town. Nothing before January 1, 1993 shall be allowed into the town.
The reason for this date is because of the insurance industry standards
regarding roof, walls, windows, siding, fire safety, wiring, sheetrock walls
(compared to paneling) and other safety features that provide a safer structure
in the event of a fire or other hazard and improves the overall life of the
house.
   C.   In addition to the age requirement for a manufactured home, minimum
conditions and standards shall be met on each and every pre-owned manufactured
home prior to being issued a zoning compliance permit. Those conditions that
are set forth in the pre-owned manufactured home minimum standards are hereby
adopted as follows:
      1.   "Pre-owned manufactured home" is defined as any manufactured home as
defined in title 47 Oklahoma Statutes that has been previously utilized as a
residential dwelling.
      2.   The following facilities shall be required:
         a.   Sanitary Facilities: Every pre-owned manufactured home shall not
contain less than a kitchen sink, lavatory, tub or shower, and a water closet
all in working condition when properly connected to an approved water and sewer
system. Every plumbing fixture and water and waste pipe shall be in sanitary
working condition free from leaks and obstructions.
         b.   Hot And Cold Water Supply: Every kitchen sink, lavatory and tub
or shower in a pre-owned manufactured home must be connected to a supply of
both hot and cold water.
         c.   Water Heating Facilities: Every pre-owned manufactured home shall
have water-heating facilities in safe, working condition.
         d.   Heating Facilities: Every pre-owned manufactured home shall have
heating facilities in safe working condition.
         e.   Un-vented fuel burning heaters shall be prohibited except for gas
heaters listed for un-vented use and the total input rating for the un-vented
heaters is less than twenty (20) BTU per hour per cubic foot of room content.
         f.   Un-vented fuel burning heaters shall be prohibited in bedrooms
and bathrooms.
         g.   In lieu of inspecting the heating facilities of a pre-owned
manufactured home, the retailer may request a heating inspection by a qualified
third party. Approval by such a person will be accepted as compliance with
those portions of the safety standards established herein which pertain to
heating systems.
         h.   Smoke Detector: Every pre-owned manufactured home shall be
provided approved listed smoke detectors installed in accordance with the
manufacturer's recommendations and listing.
         i.   Windows: Every habitable room excluding bathrooms, kitchens, and
hallways shall have at least one (1) window that can be opened, facing directly
to the outdoors.
         j.   Ventilation: Every habitable room shall have at least one (1)
window which can be opened or such device that will ventilate the room.
         k.   Electrical: Every pre-owned manufactured home electrical
components shall be in safe, working condition. Approval by a qualified third
party will be accepted as compliance with those portions of the safety
standards established herein which pertained to electrical systems.
         l.   Distribution Panels: Distribution panels shall be in compliance
with the approved listing, complete with required breakers or fuses, with all
unused openings covered with blank covers approved and listed for that purpose.
Connections shall be checked for tightness. Panels shall be accessible.
         m.   Electrical System: The electrical system (switches, receptacles,
fixtures, and the like) shall be properly installed, wired and shall be in
working condition. The pre-owned manufactured home shall be subjected to an
electrical continuity test(s) to assure that all metallic parts are properly
bonded.
         n.   Exterior Walls: The exterior of the home shall be free of loose
or rotting boards or timbers and any other conditions that might admit rain or
moisture to the interior portions of the walls or to the occupied spaces of the
pre-owned manufactured home.
         o.   Exterior Siding: The exterior siding of the pre-owned
manufactured home shall be free of rot and rust and must be uniform in
appearance.
         p.   Roofs: Roofs shall be structurally sound and have no obvious
defects, which might admit rain or cause moisture to collect on the interior
portion of the home.
         q.   Interior Floors, Walls, And Ceiling: Every floor, interior wall
and ceiling shall be in sound condition to prevent the admittance of rain or
moisture.
   D.   Installation And Inspection:
      1.   Requirements regarding installation of all manufactured home shall
be according to standards set by the Oklahoma Administrative Code title 765
Used Motor Vehicle and Parts Commission, Chapter 37 Manufactured Home
Installers, which may be amended from time to time.
      2.   The code enforcement officer or his authorized representative shall
inspect manufactured homes for compliance with these standards. Additional
inspection requirements are provided in division F, below.
      3.   A zoning compliance permit shall be required for all manufactured
homes brought into the town.
   E.   Manufactured Housing, Mobile Home Park And Travel Trailer Park, And
Subdivision Development: All manufactured housing, mobile home parks, travel
trailer parks, and subdivisions shall be developed and/or constructed in
accordance with all requirements of this code, and as follows:
      1.   Any manufactured home or mobile home that was manufactured on or
before January 1, 1993 shall not be allowed in the town.
      2.   Any manufactured home that does not meet the minimum standards set
forth in this section shall not be allowed in the town.
      3.   Any manufactured home that does not meet the specifications set
forth for the installation of all manufactured homes according to the standards
set by the Oklahoma Administrative Code title 765 Used Motor Vehicle and Parts
Commission, Chapter 37 Manufactured Home Installers, (which may be amended from
time to time), shall not be allowed in the town.
         a.   No person shall join two (2) manufactured homes, mobile homes,
recreational vehicles, or a combination thereof with a structure, or any like
units or structures to make a single dwelling unit within the town, unless said
structure is built with a common roof and enclosed by common exterior walls in
a way that would make the abode to appear to be one (1) dwelling and a zoning
compliance permit has been obtained prior to the start of construction.
         b.   All manufactured home sites must be adequately prepared with
support systems including piers, blocks, base pads, vapor guard, grading
requirements, drainage requirements, compaction of soil and all other
requirements as outlined in the Oklahoma Administrative Code title 765 Used
Motor Vehicle and Parts Commission, chapter 37 Manufactured Home Installers,
(which may be amended from time to time), and all other federal and state
requirements. This will promote the life of the manufactured home.
         c.   All manufactured homes shall meet the manufacturers'
specifications regarding safeguarding from high winds.
         d.   Wrecked, damaged or dilapidated mobile homes or manufactured
homes or recreational vehicles shall not be kept or stored within the town,
whether intended for use as a dwelling or otherwise. All such dilapidated
structures are subject to the provisions of this code.
         e.   Mobile homes and manufactured homes, occupied or unoccupied, are
considered dwelling units; any other use must be permitted only upon proper
application as a use permitted on review, as set forth in this code within
sixty (60) days. If application is not made, then the home must be removed
within a reasonable amount of time. If the owner fails to do so, then the town
may elect to have the unit towed at the owner's expense.
         f.   No person shall permanently reside in a travel trailer or
recreational vehicle, except as allowed in RV parks. Appropriate sewage
disposal shall be necessary for all recreational vehicles and travel trailers.
         g.   It is unlawful for any person to obtain, have furnished, or
receive any type of utility service without first complying with all of the
terms and conditions set forth herein.
         h.   Location Of Manufactured Homes: Unless otherwise prohibited by
the covenants and restrictions for a homeowner's association, manufactured
homes that are not located within a manufactured home park or mixed use park
and which meet zoning compliance permit standards shall be allowed for use as a
permanent dwelling unit in areas zoned AR-1, AR-2, RL-1, R-1, and R-2, as set
forth in this code.
         i.   No vehicles or trailers of any type, including, but not limited
to commercial, travel, camping trailers, hauling trailers, recreational
vehicles, manufactured homes, and mobile homes, shall be parked or stored in a
manner which impedes, blocks the view, or hinders the flow of traffic. All
vehicles parked in such a manner may be towed at the owner's expense.
   F.   Licensing And/Or Permit Requirements:
      1.   a.   Town Requirements: Persons desiring to introduce a manufactured
home into the town or to relocate a currently existing manufactured home within
the town must first have the manufactured home space ready, according to the
terms of this section, and obtain a zoning compliance permit.
         b.   The unit must pass an inspection to certify its suitability for
use prior to its arrival to or relocation within the town. The requirements set
forth in this section shall be subject to inspection and approval. Inspectors
shall be those persons who are licensed to install manufactured homes as set
forth in the Oklahoma Administrative Code, title 765 chapter 37.
         c.   A copy of the inspection shall be provided with the zoning
compliance permit application.
         d.   Requirements regarding installation of all manufactured homes
shall be according to standards set by Oklahoma Statutes, title 765 Used Motor
Vehicle and Parts Commission, chapter 37 Manufactured Homes Installers, as may
be amended from time to time.
         e.   All costs associated with inspections and installation shall be
incurred solely by the homeowner.
      2.   It shall be unlawful for any person to construct, maintain,
establish or operate a manufactured home park, mixed use park, or RV park
within the corporate limits of the town, without applying for and receiving a
permit to develop such a park as a planned unit development and meet all PUD
requirements set forth in the zoning ordinance. Applications for manufactured
home parks, RV parks, and mixed use parks shall be the same as those set forth
in PUD application and review process set forth in this code.
      3.   Modification Of Parks: If additional land is purchased to expand an
existing park, or if any existing park is expanded or altered by adding hookups
and/or lots, then the manager/operator/owner of the park must comply with all
the town ordinances, including, but not limited to applying for and receiving a
zoning compliance permit for a PUD, as set forth in this code.
      4.   If the park is permanently reduced in size by the removal of hookups
and/or lots, then in said event the park manager/ operator/owner shall notify
the town for the purpose of inspection to ensure removal of all products,
including utilities, sewage disposal system and by-products, and overall clean-
up and restoration of the premises.
      5.   Every person holding such a permit shall notify the town clerk in
writing within three (3) business days after having sold, transferred, given
away or otherwise disposed of, any interest in or control of the park, the name
and contact information of the person succeeding to the ownership or control of
such park.
      6.   Any person whose permit has been denied or suspended shall have the
right to appeal the decision to the board of adjustment as provided herein.
      7.   The town clerk shall charge and collect for each application a fee
in such sum as set by the board of trustees by motion or resolution.
   G.   Federal, State, And County Requirements:
      1.   In addition to the above stated requirements, every manager or
operator of a park shall be licensed through all of the appropriate federal,
state and county agencies, as applicable, including, but not limited to water,
sewage disposal and other required license.
      2.   Every owner, manager, and operator shall comply with any other rule
and regulation required by applicable federal, state, or county agencies,
including, but not limited to rules for water and sewage disposal. (Ord. 10-7-
3, 6-5-2017)
