10-8-1: PURPOSE AND INTENT:
   In order to protect the public health, peace, safety and welfare of the town
and its residents, this chapter is enacted to establish reasonable and uniform
limitations, safeguards, and controls for the drilling, operation and
production of oil, gas and other hydrocarbon substances within the corporate
limits of the town. The provisions set forth in this chapter shall be
considered as minimum requirements and shall not relieve any person from any
duty imposed by the law to use reasonable care and take reasonable precautions
for the safeguarding of people and the protection and noninterference with
property rights. (1994 Code § 5-901)
10-8-2: DEFINITIONS:
   All technical or oil and gas industry words or phrases used herein and not
specifically defined herein shall have those meanings customarily attributable
thereto by prudent operators in the oil and gas industry. For the purpose of
this chapter, the following definitions shall apply:
   ABANDONED WELL:
      A.   Each well in which no production casing has been run and for which
drilling or testing operations have ceased for thirty (30) consecutive days; or
      B.   Any other well for which there is no current town permit.
   ARTIFICIAL PRODUCTION: The raising of petroleum or natural gas to the
surface of the earth, by means other than natural flow.
   BOARD OF TRUSTEES: The governing body of the town.
   BUILDING: Any structure used or intended for supporting or sheltering any
use or occupancy. The term "building" shall be construed as if followed by the
words "or portions thereof".
   CORPORATION COMMISSION: The Oklahoma corporation commission.
   DELETERIOUS SUBSTANCE: Any chemical, saltwater, oil field brine, waste oil,
waste emulsified oil, basic sediment, mud or injurious substances produced or
used in the drilling, development, producing, transporting, refining and
processing of oil, gas or condensate.
   DWELLING: Any building used as an abode for people or animals.
   ENHANCED RECOVERY: An operation in which fluid or energy is introduced into
a source of supply for the purpose of facilitating recovery therefrom.
   HIGHWAY: The entire width between the boundary line of every way publicly
maintained when any part thereof is open to the use of the public for purposes
of vehicular travel.
   MAYOR: The chief elected official of the town.
   MOTOR VEHICLE: Any automobile, truck, truck-tractor, trailer or semitrailer
or any motor bus or any self-propelled vehicle not operated or driven upon
fixed rails or tracks.
   OIL AND GAS INSPECTOR: That person, firm or corporation designated by the
town to enforce the provisions of this chapter, or authorized representatives.
   OPERATOR: Any person who owns or who has ever owned any working interest in
any well bore located within the corporate limits of the town, and any person
who operates or who has ever operated any well on a contractual basis for any
working interest owner.
   PERMITTEE: The person to whom is issued a permit or permits under the terms
of this chapter.
   PERSON: Means and includes any person, firm, partnership, association,
corporation, trust, cooperative, or other type of organization.
   POLLUTION: The contamination or other alteration of the physical, chemical,
or biological properties of any natural waters of the town, or such discharge
of any liquid, gaseous or solid substance into any water of the town as will or
is likely to create a nuisance or render such waters harmful or detrimental or
injurious to the public health, safety, or welfare; to domestic, commercial,
industrial, agricultural, recreational or other beneficial uses; or to
livestock, animals or aquatic life.
   PRESSURE MAINTENANCE: An operation by which gas, water or other fluids are
injected into a supply of oil to maintain pressure or retard pressure.
   STATE: The state of Oklahoma, its branches, departments, agencies, boards or
the officers thereof.
   TOWN: The town of Wayne, Oklahoma.
   TREATABLE WATER: Surface and subsurface water in its natural state which may
or may not require treatment to be useful for human consumption, and contains
less than ten thousand (10,000) ppm total dissolved solids or five thousand
(5,000) ppm chlorides.
   WATER, WATERS OF THE TOWN OR TOWN WATER: Streams, lakes, ponds, marshes,
watercourses, waterways, wells, springs, irrigation systems, drainage systems,
and all other bodies or accumulations of water, surface and underground,
natural or artificial, public or private, which are contained within, flow
through or border upon the town or any portion thereof.
   WELL: Unless specifically qualified, any hole or holes, bore or bores to any
depth for the purpose of producing and recovering any oil, gas or liquified
petroleum matter or deleterious substance, or for the injection or disposal of
any of the foregoing into the surface of the earth, by natural flow, petroleum
or natural gas. (1994 Code § 5-902)
10-8-3: APPLICATION OF PROVISIONS:
   A.   This chapter shall apply to oil and gas and any other unnamed group
operating within the town, as defined and extended by law.
   B.   This chapter shall apply to any person drilling an original well,
reentering an abandoned well, conducting natural or artificial production
projects or operations, or maintaining a disposal well within the town on
December 1, 1984, and every such person shall have no longer than ninety (90)
days to come into compliance with this chapter. Provided, that:
      1.   No initial permit fees shall be charged such person as would
otherwise apply; and
      2.   No penalties shall be sought against any activity violative of this
chapter where such activity preexisted the adoption of this chapter and was
otherwise in compliance with the applicable state law, rules, regulations,
standards or directives. (1994 Code § 5-904)
10-8-4: STATE RULES AND REGULATIONS APPLY:
   All of the general rules and regulations of the corporation commission are
hereby incorporated into this chapter and may be enforced by the oil and gas
inspector. Any violation of any of these rules and regulations shall be subject
to enforcement and penalties as provided for in section 10-8-28 of this
chapter. In the event of any conflicts between the provisions of this chapter
and the corporation commission rules and regulations, the more restrictive
provision shall apply. (1994 Code § 5-904)
10-8-5: OIL AND GAS INSPECTOR:
   A.   The mayor shall designate a qualified person, firm or corporation as an
oil and gas inspector, whose duty it shall be to enforce the provisions of this
chapter. Since information of considerable value may be made available to town
officials and to the oil and gas inspector, confidentiality of this information
is of prime importance. Thus, all officials and agents of the town shall hold
such information in strict confidence. All information that is not otherwise
public information shall be considered the sole property of the operating
company and other working interest owners, and such property shall not be
released in fact or in summary to any other party except as required under
these regulations. Any infraction of this trust shall be considered by the town
board. If the oil and gas inspector is involved, the infraction shall be
grounds for terminating any and all contracts between the town and the oil and
gas inspector.
   B.   The oil and gas inspector shall have the authority to issue such orders
or directives as are required to carry out the intent and purpose of this
chapter and its particular provisions. Failure to abide by any such order or
directive shall be a violation of this chapter.
   C.   The oil and gas inspector shall have the authority to go upon and
inspect any premises covered by the terms of this chapter to ascertain whether
this chapter and the applicable laws, rules, regulations, standards or
directives of the state are being complied with. Failure to permit access to
the oil and gas inspector shall be deemed a violation of this chapter.
   D.   The oil and gas inspector shall have the authority to request and
receive any records specified in this chapter relating to the status or
condition of any well or project or the appurtenances therewith in the town.
Failure to provide any such requested material shall be deemed a violation of
this chapter.
   E.   The operator shall notify the oil and gas inspector sufficiently in
advance as to permit the oil and gas inspector to be present when the following
work is performed:
      1.   Running and cementing of conductor casing;
      2.   Pressure testing of BOP stack choke and choke manifold after initial
installation;
      3.   Running and cementing of surface casing;
      4.   Running bond logs;
      5.   Running and cementing intermediate casing;
      6.   Performing BOP drills when requested;
      7.   Performing actuation and pressure test of BOP stack when requested;
      8.   Performing formation leak off test;
      9.   Setting cement plugs while abandoning wells;
      10.   Installing tubing and packer where packer is required;
      11.   Working over or recompleting wells;
      12.   Abandoning wells;
      13.   Performing pressure tests on casing;
      14.   Repair of any casing leaks in wells;
      15.   Repair of any leaks in pipelines;
      16.   Cleanup of any spillage of oil, gas, or saltwater;
      17.   Running and cementing of production casing;
      18.   Annular mechanical integrity tests;
      19.   Collection of all samples from monitoring wells; and
      20.   Annual test of pipelines and conduits.
   F.   Additional duties of the oil and gas inspector are as follows:
      1.   Examine the application to the town and make recommendations to the
town board on all matters related to oil and gas operations.
      2.   Coordinate activities with the operating companies, including:
         a.   Assist with making applications to the town by providing
information, forms, and definitions;
         b.   Inspect all drilling and construction operations; and
         c.   Advise and approve changes where options are allowed in this
chapter. For example, to approve a contingency plan to obtain a degasser in
lieu of requiring one to be installed and in use (subsection
10-8-16D of this chapter).
(1994 Code § 5-942)
10-8-6: PERMIT REQUIREMENTS:
   A.   Permit Required: It is unlawful and an offense for any person acting
for himself or acting as agent, servant, employee, subcontractor, or
independent contractor of any other person to drill a well within the town, or
to work upon or assist in any way in the production or operation of any such
well, without a permit having first been issued by the authority of the oil and
gas inspector in accordance with this chapter. Only producing wells will be
considered for a permit. (1994 Code §§ 5-904, 5-905)
   B.   Application For Permit: Every application for a permit to drill an
original well or to reenter an abandoned well shall be in writing, signed by
the applicant or some person duly authorized to sign same on his behalf, and it
shall be filed with the oil and gas inspector and be accompanied by the filing
fee as set by the town board by motion or resolution 1 . No application shall
request a permit to drill more than one well. The application shall contain
full information required by the oil and gas inspector, including the
following:
      1.   Name and address of applicant and date of application;
      2.   The name and address of each working interest owner showing the
percent of interest held in the well. This listing must be updated yearly;
      3.   A block map of the ten (10) acres surrounding the drill site,
including thereon the location of the proposed well and distance therefrom to
all existing dwelling houses, buildings, or other structures designed for the
occupancy of human beings or animals and the owners thereof as shown by the
current tax rolls in the county treasurer's office, within six hundred feet
(600') of any such well, and the location of all existing oil, gas or
freshwater wells within the ten (10) acre tract;
      4.   The names of the surface and surface lease owners;
      5.   A copy of the approved drilling permit from the corporation
commission and a copy of the staking plat shall be filed with the town prior to
issuance of the municipal permit; (1994 Code § 5-905)
      6.   A drilling prognosis, to specify in detail the amount, weight, and
size of conductor pipe and surface pipe and the procedures to be used for
cementing such. All cement plans shall show the weight of cement to be used and
maximum pressure allowable to prevent fracturing. Plugging procedures to be
used in the event production is not established shall also be specified and
shall be in accordance with state corporation commission (see subsection
10-8-16F of this chapter); (1994 Code § 5-905; amd. 2004 Code)
      7.   A statement of the provisions for water for the drilling rig;
      8.   The proposed mud program, including statements from a prospective
supplier as to the toxic nature of all materials recommended in the plan. The
material safety data sheets shall also include handling and disposal
recommendations;
      9.   A written plan for disposal of deleterious substances produced
during the drilling operations and any deleterious substances produced as a
result of production from the well. This plan shall include the method of
transportation and the name of the transporter or transport contractor for the
deleterious substances and the permit number, name and phone number of the
operator company and location of the permitted disposal site;
      10.   The permittee shall maintain reports of volumes disposed of and
receipts for the disposal of same. If the disposal well is within the limits of
the town site, records of injection pressures shall also be maintained. These
records shall be open for inspection during normal office hours and submitted
to the oil and gas inspector as part of the yearly review;
      11.   A spill contingency plan. See section
10-8-7 of this chapter;
      12.   A ground water monitoring plan. See section
10-8-8 of this chapter;
      13.   A surface water diversion plan. See section
10-8-9 of this chapter;
      14.   A plan for performing the yearly mechanical integrity tests;
      15.   Plans for abandoning the well. This plan shall designate a method
of filling the well bore above the bottom plug to the base of the surface plug
with a material of sufficient compressive strength to support the surface plug.
The surface plug shall extend from two hundred feet (200') below the base of
the surface casing to the cut off point below flow depth. Normal corporation
commission rules shall apply except the requirements of filling the well bore
with a solid material and the length of the surface plug;
      16.   The name and address of the person within the state of Oklahoma
upon whom service of process upon applicant (or operator company, if different)
may be made within the state; and in the case of any nonresident person who has
no such service agent within the state, there shall be attached to the
application the designation of such a service agent resident in Oklahoma
County, Oklahoma, and a consent that service of summons may be made upon such
person in any action to enforce any of the obligations of the applicant
hereunder;
      17.   A verification of the above information by the applicant hereunder;
and
      18.   Power of attorney for each authorized signatories.
   C.   Additional Information Required: Other information in addition to that
required in the original permit application and actions necessary following
permit approval are:
      1.   Prior to spudding - laboratory reports on ground water samples. See
section
10-8-8 of this chapter;
      2.   Thirty (30) days prior to commencement of operation, notice of same
and application for trucking routes. See subsection
10-8-16A of this chapter;
      3.   Prior to final approval of the permit, all required bonds and
certifications of insurance must be submitted. See section
10-8-11 of this chapter;
      4.   Thirty (30) days prior to commencement of any excavations or off
site construction, any requirements under section
10-8-14 of this chapter; and
      5.   Five (5) working days prior to the actual commencement of work, the
town clerk-treasurer must be notified. The phone number is: (405) 396-2899. The
address is: P.O. Box 268, Arcadia, Oklahoma 73007. Failure to provide notice
shall be considered a violation, and penalties shall be assessed.
   D.   Specific Well Requirements: Refer to the following sections for
specific well requirements:
      1.   Directional wells. See subsection
10-8-10A of this chapter;
      2.   Reentry of abandoned wells. See subsection
10-8-10B of this chapter; and
      3.   Conduits on streets and alleys. See section
10-8-14 of this chapter. (1994 Code § 5-905)
   E.   Issuance Or Denial Of Permit:
      1.   The oil and gas inspector's office, within thirty (30) days after
the filing of an application for permit under this chapter, shall determine
whether or not the application complies in all respects with the provisions of
this chapter and applicable federal and state law and, if it does, shall
recommend to the mayor and the board of trustees that the permit be issued. The
oil and gas inspector shall have fifteen (15) days to review the permit. Each
permit issued under the terms of this chapter shall:
         a.   By reference, have incorporated therein all the provisions of
this chapter with the same force and effect as if this chapter were copied
verbatim therein;
         b.   By reference, have incorporated therein all the provisions of
applicable state law and the rules, regulations and standards adopted in
accordance therewith relating to the protection of human beings, animals, and
natural resources;
         c.   Specify that the term of the permit shall be for a period of one
year from the date of issuance thereof, and for like periods thereafter upon
the successful inspection of the permittee's well and operations, as is
provided for elsewhere herein;
         d.   Specify such conditions imposed by the oil and gas inspector as
are by this chapter authorized; and
         e.   Specify that no actual operations shall be commenced until the
permittee shall file and have approved the required bonds and certificate of
insurance in the appropriate amounts as provided for elsewhere herein.
      2.   If the permit is issued, it shall, in two (2) originals, be signed
by the oil and gas inspector and the permittee and, when so signed, shall
constitute the permittee's license to drill and operate in the town and the
contractual obligation of the permittee to comply with the terms of such
permit, such bonds as are required, and applicable state law, rules,
regulations, standards and directives. One executed original copy of the permit
shall be retained by the oil and gas inspector; the other shall be retained by
the permittee and shall be kept available for inspection by any town or state
law enforcement official who shall demand to see same.
      3.   If the permit is refused, or if the applicant notifies the oil and
gas inspector in writing that he does not elect to accept the permit as
tendered and wishes to withdraw his application, or if the bonds of the
applicant are not approved, then, upon the happening of any of the events, the
cash fee filed with the application shall be refunded to the applicant; except,
that there shall be retained therefrom by the town the sum of five hundred
dollars ($500.00) as a processing fee. (1994 Code § 5-913)
   F.   Fee And Renewal Of Permit: The permittee under subsection A of this
section shall pay to the town an annual and renewal fee as set out in section
2-2-8 of this code. The oil and gas inspector shall inspect such conduits to
ensure the public safety. No permit issued under subsection A of this section
shall be renewed if the conduit or any part thereof covered by such permit is
in an unsafe condition. (1994 Code § 5-936; amd. 2004 Code)

  2-2-8 of this code.
10-8-7: SPILL CONTINGENCY AND CONTROL PLAN:
   A.   Submission Of Plan; Required Information:
      1.   In addition, the permittee or applicant shall submit a deleterious
substance spill contingency plan that will be followed in reporting, cleanup,
and prevention of the spread of any pollution resulting from a deleterious
substance spill which might occur during drilling or production activities. The
plan shall include the description of procedures, persons, personnel,
equipment, and materials which will be used in combating any such spill. The
plan shall address two (2) degrees of response effort:
         a.   A deleterious substance spill resulting in pollutants being
carried outside the dike surrounding the well or facility site; and
         b.   A deleterious substance spill resulting in pollutants being
carried into surface waste.
      2.   The deleterious substance spill control plan shall include
provisions to assure that full resource capability is known and can be
committed during a spill, including the identification and inventory of
applicable equipment and materials which are available locally and regionally.
      3.   The deleterious substance spill control plan shall include
establishment of procedures for the purpose of early detection and timely
notification of a spill, including a current list of names, telephone numbers
and addresses of the responsible persons and alternates on call to receive
notification of a spill; and the names, telephone numbers and addresses of
regulatory organizations and agencies to be notified when a spill is
discovered. The plan shall include predesignation of a spill response
coordinator who is charged with the responsibility and is delegated
commensurate authority for directing and coordinating response operations. The
plan shall also include provisions for disposal of recovered spill materials.
      4.   The plan shall address the installation of a secondary protection
dike located between the well or facility site and any water to be protected.
When the oil and gas inspector considers a secondary dike to be effective and
practical, the permittee shall construct and maintain a secondary protection
dike at some point downslope of the well site, crossing any drainage channels
in such a manner that any fluid flowing downstream from the well or facility
site could be trapped. This secondary dike shall contain a simple slide gate or
valve, sized to pass surface water, which can be closed by one man. The gate
shall be left open at all times except when and if control of the well is lost,
in which event, the gate in the secondary dike shall be immediately closed and
left closed until control of the well is regained. If any contaminants have
entered the secondary dike pool, the pool shall be pumped dry and the pool area
cleaned of contaminants before the gate in the secondary dike is reopened.
   B.   Approval Of Plan; Annual Review: Approval of the plan by the oil and
gas inspector shall be a requirement for obtaining a permit. The plan shall be
reviewed annually by the permittee and revised as appropriate to compensate for
any changes in conditions at the well or facility site or at the area to be
protected. The permittee shall submit a plan or revised plan to the oil and gas
inspector on an annual basis. (1994 Code § 5-906)
10-8-8: GROUND WATER MONITORING INFORMATION:
   The applicant shall submit the following information concerned with
monitoring possible pollution of treatable and freshwater in the area of the
drill site. Domestic and public water supply wells located within a radius of
one-quarter (1/4) mile of any well shall be tested prior to beginning
production or injection operations and thereafter annually for the presence of
deleterious substances. The monitoring plan shall consist of:
   A.   Map: A map showing the designated monitoring wells. If a number of
wells exist in the designated area, only one up gradient well and two (2) down
gradient wells will be required. If contamination is envisioned or if a failure
in mechanical integrity has been detected, two (2) down gradient monitoring
wells shall be drilled and sampled on a recurring basis.
   B.   Sampling And Analysis Plan: A written sampling and analysis (S&A) plan
which includes procedures for sample collection, preservation, and the chain of
custody.
      1.   Samples:
         a.   Samples will be taken prior to spudding the well. The oil and gas
inspector shall be given adequate notification of the sampling date and time.
The following test shall be performed on the samples by an approved third party
laboratory:
            (1)   Total dissolved solids; and
            (2)   Total chlorides.
         b.   The results of this sampling shall be filed with the oil and gas
inspector and shall function as a base line to determine if pollution has
occurred.
      2.   Analyses: Once a year, the oil and gas inspector shall be notified
and samples taken and turned over to a qualified third party laboratory
(approved by the oil and gas inspector) for analysis. Such testing is the
responsibility of the permittee and at the permittee's expense and is to be
conducted by a party approved by the oil and gas inspector. (1994 Code § 5-907)
10-8-9: SURFACE WATER DIVERSION INFORMATION:
   A.   The applicant shall submit maps and drawings showing the means to be
used for diverting surface water from the drilling/production site. Any
structures required shall be sized to handle a ten (10) year SCS type II storm
without being toppled. Volumes shall be based on the SCS's graphical method.
Any changes in the surface runoff patterns must be directed from the area
without causing erosion. The applicant shall submit:
      1.   A reproduction of a map (topographic sheet) showing the drainage
area and proposed changes in drainage patterns.
      2.   All calculations indicating that the proposed patterns will not
create erosional velocities.
   B.   No specific requirements exist if the drainage area is less than ten
(10) acres and the slope across the drill site is less than five feet (5') per
one hundred feet (100').
   C.   If erosion is found to be occurring on the site or on adjacent areas
affected by production operations, additional corrective and protective
measures will be required. (1994 Code § 5-908)
10-8-10: DIRECTIONAL AND ABANDONED WELLS:
   A.   Directional Wells: Directional wells are those wells purposely deviated
from vertical. Directional wells must be identified in the application for
permit to drill. Both the surface and target locations must be identified. Upon
completion of the drilling of the well, a copy of the directional survey report
shall be filed with the oil and gas inspector. (1994 Code § 5-909)
   B.   Reentry Of Abandoned Wells: Where the application is one for the
reentry of an abandoned well, the application shall contain all the information
required of a new well. That application shall also provide all of the
following information:
      1.   The condition of the abandoned well at the time the application is
filed;
      2.   The depth to which it is proposed such well shall be deepened;
      3.   The casing program to be used in connection with the proposed
deepening;
      4.   The tests which will be run on the existing casing strings to show
that the casing strings meet the same requirements as for an original well.
Include any proposed remedial cementing. The oil and gas inspector shall be on
location for all such tests; and
      5.   VDL and PETTM/CETTM bond logs over the length of the existing
surface casing and extension to this casing shall be submitted. The oil and gas
inspector shall be on site when the log is run. Two (2) copies are to be
provided to the oil and gas inspector. (1994 Code § 5-910)
10-8-11: INSURANCE REQUIREMENTS:
   A.   Insurance Required:
      1.   Prior to the issuance of any permit and at least twenty (20) days
prior to the expiration date of any existing policy, the applicant shall
deposit with the town clerk-treasurer a cash or surety in an amount sufficient
to ensure compliance with all requirements of this chapter by the owner, his
driller, agents and employees during the drilling and operation of the well.
This surety shall be conditioned upon plugging of the well, removing all
pipelines, foundations, fences, vessels, tanks, pumps, electrical poles and
other facilities and the restoration of the land surface. Such plugging and
restoration shall be performed within ninety (90) days of the abandonment in
accordance with the rules and of the Oklahoma corporation commission and this
chapter. Such sureties will be prepaid with an annual renewal date
corresponding to the yearly well review. Since the surety bond must be in
effect as long as the well produces, the bond must be written as continuous or
until canceled. Upon noncompliance with the above listed conditions, the cash
amount or the surety bond shall be forfeited and shall provide for the plugging
of the well or the restoration of the land surface.
      2.   Prior to the issuance of any permit, the applicant shall also
deposit copies of the following insurance policies issued by a corporate
insurer licensed to do business in the state of Oklahoma: (1994 Code § 5-912)
         a.   A policy containing coverage for contamination and pollution of
surface or subterranean streams, watercourses, lakes or public or private water
supplies in an amount not less than two million dollars ($2,000,000.00).
         b.   A policy or policies of standard comprehensive public liability
insurance, including contractual liability covering bodily injuries and
property damage, in the face amount of not less than two million dollars
($2,000,000.00).
         c.   A worker's compensation policy in the face amount as may be
advised by the town attorney.
         d.   A blowout protection policy in the face amount of two million
dollars ($2,000,000.00) or more. (1994 Code § 5-912; amd. 2004 Code)
   B.   Town As Beneficiary: The operator may choose to have the town as an
additional beneficiary on all policies or, at the operator's election, pay for
a master policy for the town. In either case, the protection offered the town
and its environment shall be the same.
   C.   Cancellation Of Policies: The insurance policy or policies shall not be
canceled without written notice to the oil and gas inspector at least thirty
(30) days prior to the effective date of such cancellation. In the event the
policy or policies are canceled, the permits granted to that operating company
shall immediately thereupon terminate without any action on the part of the oil
and gas inspector or the town. The permittee's rights to operation shall cease
until permittee files additional insurance as provided herein. Operation may
not begin until insurance policy or policies have been approved.
   D.   Expiration Of Policies: The insurance policy or policies shall not be
allowed to expire. In the event the policy or policies do expire, all permits
granted to the operator shall immediately thereupon terminate without any
action on the part of the oil and gas inspector or the town. The permittee's
rights to operation shall cease until the permittee files additional insurance
and the policy or policies have been approved. (1994 Code § 5-912)
10-8-12: LOCATION RESTRICTIONS:
   A.   Near Residences: No permit shall be issued for the drilling of an
original well or the reentry of an abandoned well at any location which is
nearer than four hundred feet (400') to any permanent residence or commercial
building, or which is closer than four hundred feet (400') to a producing
freshwater well.
   B.   Location In Or Obstruction Of Public Ways: No well shall be drilled,
and no permit shall be issued for any well to be drilled, at any location which
is within any of the streets or alleys of the town; and no street or alley
shall be blocked or encumbered or closed in any drilling or production
operation except with the written approval of the oil and gas inspector, and
then only temporarily. (1994 Code § 5-904)
10-8-13: POLLUTION PREVENTION AND CONTROL:
   A.   Pollution Prevention Required; Prohibitions:
      1.   All permittees, operators, contractors, drillers, service companies,
pipe pulling and salvaging contractors, or other persons shall at all times
conduct their operations and drill equipment, operate, produce, plug and
abandon all wells drilled for oil or gas, service wells or exploratory wells
(including seismic, core and stratigraphic holes) in a manner that will prevent
pollution and the migration of oil, gas, saltwater or other substances from one
stratum into another, including the Garber-Wellington aquifer or any other
freshwater bearing formation. Pollution of surface or subsurface freshwater by
deleterious substances used in connection with the exploration, drilling,
producing, refining, transporting or processing of oil or gas is hereby
prohibited. (1994 Code § 5-903)
      2.   No person shall deposit, drain or divert into or upon any public
highway, street or alley, drainage ditch, storm drain, sewer, gutter, paving,
creek, river, lake or lagoon, any oil or oily liquid with petroleum content or
any mud, rotary mud, sand, water or saltwater or in any manner permit by
seepage, overflow, deliberate release or otherwise any of such substances to
escape from any property owned, leased or controlled by such person to flow or
be carried into or upon any public highway, street or alley, drainage ditch,
storm drain, sewer, gutter, paving, creek, river, lake or lagoon, within the
town. (1994 Code §§ 5-903, 5-927)
      3.   All oil operations, drilling and production operations shall be
conducted in such a manner as to eliminate, as far as practicable, dust, noise,
vibrations or noxious odors and shall be in accordance with the best accepted
practices incident to exploration for, drilling for and production of oil, gas
and other hydrocarbon substances. Proven technological improvements in
exploration, drilling and production methods shall be adopted as they become
available, if capable of reducing factors of nuisance and annoyance.
      4.   In the completion of oil and gas, injection, disposal or surface
well, where acidizing or fracturing processes are used, no oil, gas or other
deleterious substances or pollutants shall be permitted to pollute any surface
or subsurface freshwaters.
      5.   In swabbing and bailing or purging a well, all deleterious
substances removed from the bore hole shall be placed in appropriate tanks, and
no substances shall be permitted to pollute any surface or subsurface
freshwaters.
      6.   In the event a rupture, break or opening occurs in the surface or
production casing, the permittee or operator or drilling contractor shall take
immediate action to reseal it and shall report the incident to the oil and gas
inspector promptly.
      7.   No permittee or any other person shall burn any trash or debris
within the town limits.
      8.   Toxic chemicals must be stored in their original containers. The oil
and gas inspector shall have the authority to limit the quantity and type of
the substances stored or used in these areas. The permittee shall provide a
list of all toxic chemicals along with the national safety data sheets on
products used or stored in these areas prior to their use. Continued use or
storage shall be reported in the survey for each year.
      9.   Miscellaneous regulations are as follows:
         a.   All leaking pumps, stuffing boxes, lines, tanks and connections
shall be promptly corrected and shall not be used while leaking. Drip pans
shall be installed where minor leaks are likely to occur;
         b.   All oil, water or deleterious substances from wet strings of
tubing shall be drained into steel tanks;
         c.   All oil cellars and oil pumps shall be promptly pumped out;
         d.   All leases shall be kept clean of refuse and other deleterious
matter; and
         e.   All ditches shall be kept clean and in a good state of repair.
(1994 Code § 5-927)
   B.   Enforcement Of Provisions:
      1.   The oil and gas inspector shall have the authority to require the
immediate shutting in or closing of any well if he finds that there exists,
within a one hundred foot (100') radius of any well, any gas or gasoline vapor
in a quantity sufficient to constitute, in his judgment or in the judgment of
the town fire chief, a fire hazard. The well shall remain shut or closed in
until the hazard and its cause are removed. (1994 Code § 5-927; amd. 2004 Code)
      2.   In the event of any leakage or spillage of any pollutant or
deleterious substance, whatever the cause thereof, the permittee shall cause
the oil and gas inspector to be notified thereof promptly. The spill
contingency plan will be activated. If, in the judgment of the oil and gas
inspector, such leakage or spillage represents a potential environmental
hazard, he may issue whatever corrective orders he deems appropriate, and
additionally may require the appropriate testing of the surface and subsurface
for pollutant incursion, the cost of such test or tests to be borne by the
permittee. (1994 Code § 5-927)
10-8-14: OFF SITE CONSTRUCTION; CONDUITS:
   A.   Permit Requirements:
      1.   Permit Required: No permittee shall make any excavations or
construct any lines for the conveyance of fuel, water, or minerals on, under or
through areas of the town without first having obtained a permit upon
application to the town board.
      2.   Application For Permit: The application shall be in writing, signed
by the applicant or some person duly authorized to sign same on his behalf, and
it shall be filed with the oil and gas inspector and be accompanied by the
filing fee as set by the town board by motion or resolution 1 . No application
shall request a permit to install more than one line. The oil and gas inspector
shall prescribe the forms to be used for such application and the information
to accompany it.
      3.   Issuance Or Denial Of Permit: The town board shall, within twenty
(20) days of receipt of the properly executed application, either grant or deny
the request. The granting of any such permit shall not be construed to be the
granting of a franchise.
   B.   Operation Restrictions; Conduit Specifications: In no case shall gas or
air be transported through any pipeline at a working pressure in excess of one
thousand (1,000) pounds. A pipe used for the transportation of air or gas
ranging from six hundred (600) to one thousand (1,000) pounds' pressure shall
be from two (2) to ten inches (10") in outer diameter and shall be seamless API
pipe with welded or screwed couplings. Lines carrying deleterious substances
and low pressure gas (psi 100) may be nonmetallic. Any new or replacement pipe
shall be covered and not less than forty eight inches (48") below the regular
ground level and shall be placed in conduit whenever laid under any portion of
a street, roadway, alley or driveway ordinarily used for heavy vehicular
traffic. These lines shall be tested before being used and every twelve (12)
months during the annual well inspection cycle. The test shall be made with
water. Pipe shall stand a pressure of two thousand (2,000) pounds. Nonmetallic
lines will be tested to eighty percent (80%) of the manufacturer's state
internal yield strength.
   C.   Safety Precautions; Warning Devices: The permittee will erect and
maintain all barricades, lights and traffic control devices needed. The
permittee shall notify "Oklahoma One Call" at least three (3) working days
before digging:
      "Call Okie" at 1-800-522-6543 or in Oklahoma City 405-840-5032.
   D.   Designation Of Conduits: The location of all new or replacement
pipelines shall be marked by the owners thereof or by the person installing
such pipelines as follow:
      1.   Marker signs shall be placed at all locations where pipelines cross
property boundary lines and at each side of a public street or road right of
way which the pipeline crosses;
      2.   The top of all marker signs shall be a minimum of four feet (4')
above ground level, and the support post shall be painted yellow;
      3.   All marker signs shall be a minimum of twelve inches (12") square
and shall be marked as "gas pipeline", "oil pipeline", or "S.W. pipeline" as
may be applicable; and
      4.   All marker signs shall contain the name of the owner of the
pipeline.
   E.   As Built Drawings: A set of as built drawings of all lines constructed
shall be submitted. These drawings shall show the location of all lines, the
depth of burial, the materials used in construction, and the location of all
valves, vents, drips and fittings of significance.
   F.   Restoration Of Area: The permittee shall restore all surfaces to
preconstruction conditions.
   G.   Inspections: The oil and gas inspector shall inspect all pressure lines
in use at any well or at any project to assure that tubing, fittings, equipment
or connections are reasonably tight, safe and free from leaks. (1994 Code § 5-
911)
 
Notes
1 See section
  2-2-8 of this code.
10-8-15: SEISMIC EXPLORATION:
   It is unlawful for any person, firm or corporation to conduct seismic
exploration or related activities in the town limits without first having filed
an application for a permit with the oil and gas inspector and paying a fee,
based on each mile of cable proposed to be laid, in such sum as set by the town
board by motion or resolution 1 . The application shall be accompanied by a
chart or map outlining proposed activities and time frame. (1994 Code § 5-915)
 
Notes
1 See section
  2-2-8 of this code.
10-8-16: OPERATION AND CONSTRUCTION OF WELLS:
   A.   Determination Of Routes To Well Site; Notice Of Operation:
      1.   At least thirty (30) days prior to the actual commencement of any
operations at the well site, the permittee shall notify the oil and gas
inspector in writing of the proposed date for commencement of such operations.
Such notification shall also contain the following information:
         a.   The permittee shall identify the maximum length, width and weight
of any motor vehicles and the maximum weight of the load to be carried by any
motor vehicles to be used in traveling to and from the well site; and
         b.   The permittee shall submit a complete list of the proposed routes
to and from the well site for all motor vehicles to be used to travel to and
from the well site. Such list shall identify any and all streets and highways
within the limits of the town proposed to be used by such motor vehicles in
traveling to and from the well site.
      2.   The oil and gas inspector may also require any additional
information which he deems necessary to evaluate the proposed routes.
      3.   Upon receipt of the notification required under subsections A1a and
A1b of this section, the oil and gas inspector shall have fifteen (15) days to
review the information submitted by the permittee.
      4.   Following review of the information submitted by the permittee, the
oil and gas inspector shall prepare a written order which either approves or
disapproves the routes to and from the well site as proposed by the permittee.
If the oil and gas inspector disapproves of all or part of the proposed routes,
then such inspector shall designate alternate routes which are acceptable. If
the permittee disagrees with the routes as designated by the oil and gas
inspector, then he shall have such right of appeal to the town board.
      5.   During all drilling and production activities for the particular
well, all motor vehicles used by any person to travel to and from the well site
shall be restricted to the streets and highways approved by the oil and gas
inspector as appropriate routes to and from the well site.
      6.   The oil and gas inspector shall have the power and authority to
amend the order designating routes to and from the well site upon his own
initiative or upon application by the permittee. (1994 Code § 5-916)
   B.   Drilling And Reentry Operations:
      1.   Well control requirements are as follows:
         a.   From the time drilling operations are commenced until the well is
completed or abandoned, the senior on site representative of the operator or
the drilling contractor's drillers and tool pushers shall have current
certification in well control through satisfactory completion of a U.S. mineral
management service approved well control school. These individuals shall
provide proof of having completed the prescribed course upon the oil and gas
inspector's request. The operator's representative and the drilling
contractor's tool pusher shall be available in a reasonably short period of
time to assist with any well control problems which may develop. In no instance
shall any person without well control certification be allowed to perform the
responsibilities of the above referenced positions, unless he is performing his
duties under the direct, on site supervision of an appropriately certified
individual;
         b.   The following information shall be posted on the rig floor at all
times after drilling is commenced:
            (1)   Procedure for closing in the well using blowout preventers
when:
               (A)   The operation is drilling or circulating with the bit on
bottom; and
               (B)   The operation is tripping the drill string. (1994 Code §
5-917)
            (2)   Prerecorded data as required by the corporation commission.
(1994 Code § 5-917; amd. 2004 Code)
            Posted data must be revised as conditions change. The circulating
pump rates and pressures must be rechecked and the posted data revised at least
once each twenty four (24) hours when drilling; and
         c.   The oil and gas inspector shall have access to all information
pertaining to a well drilled under these rules.
      2.   It is unlawful and an offense for any person to use or operate, in
connection with the drilling, reentry or reworking of any well within the town,
any wooden derrick or any steam powered rig, and all engines shall be equipped
with adequate mufflers. Permitting any drilling rig or derrick to remain on the
premises or drilling site for a period of longer than sixty (60) days after
completion or abandonment of a well is hereby prohibited.
      3.   All drilling, reentry and operations at any well performed under
this chapter shall be conducted in accordance with the best practices of the
reasonably prudent operator. All casing, valves, and blowout preventers,
drilling fluid, tubing, bradenhead, Christmas tree and well head connections
shall be of a type and quality consistent with the best practices of such
reasonably prudent operator. Setting and cementing casing and running drill
stem tests shall be performed in a manner and at a time consistent with the
best practices of such reasonably prudent operator. Any permittee under this
chapter shall observe and follow the recommendations or regulations of the
American petroleum institute and the corporation commission, except in those
instances that are specifically addressed by this chapter.
      4.   It is unlawful and an offense for any person to move or cause to be
moved the drilling rig from a well until the hole has been cased or properly
plugged, unless written permission to do so is obtained from the oil and gas
inspector.
      5.   Pit regulations are as follows:
         a.   Steel mud circulating pits shall be used. Such pits and contents
shall be removed from the premises and the drilling site within thirty (30)
days after completion of the well. Earthen pits will be allowed only as
temporary emergency pits, reserve pits or catch basins. Catch basin pits shall
be used only for the purpose of catching any deleterious substance runoff and
shall be not greater than three hundred twenty (320) cubic feet.
         b.   All such earthen pits must be approved in writing by the oil and
gas inspector. No earthen pit shall be constructed or maintained so as to
receive water from a watershed, and the fluid level of each earthen pit shall
be maintained at all times at least twelve (12) vertical inches below the
discharge of the pit.
         c.   Also, emergency pits shall be emptied as soon as the emergency is
over, and all such pits shall be emptied and then leveled within fifteen (15)
days after completion of the emergency. The oil and gas inspector shall be
notified by phone and in writing of each use of an emergency pit.
         d.   The oil and gas inspector shall be notified in writing whenever
an earthen pit is abandoned.
      6.   All open hole formation testing shall be done during daylight hours,
with advance notification thereof made to the oil and gas inspector, adequate
to enable him to be present if he so chooses. Fluid removed from the well
during testing must be flowed or pumped into steel pits or tanks and promptly
removed from the location at the conclusion of testing. Temporary catch bins
can be used, if necessary. (1994 Code § 5-917)
   C.   Loss Of Well Control: In the event any well blows out or becomes out of
control, the operator shall immediately notify the oil and gas inspector by
telephone or personal contact, and the oil and gas inspector and his agents
shall have full access to the well site and surrounding area. All condensate,
oil, mud, saltwater, and other wastes from the well shall be blocked off and
shall not be permitted to enter the reservoir or surface streams. The operator
will anticipate the worst possible blowout or overflow conditions, and shall
have readily available at all times sufficient material and equipment to
prevent pollutants from reaching the surface under any conceivable conditions.
The permittee conducting operations shall be solely responsible for all damages
and shall restore the surface to its original condition. Reference is made to
section
10-8-7 of this chapter. (1994 Code § 5-918)
   D.   Blow Out Prevention Equipment: Blowout preventers and related well
control equipment shall be installed, used and tested in a manner necessary to
prevent blowouts. Prior to drilling below the surface casing and until drilling
operations are completed, blowout prevention equipment shall be installed and
maintained ready for use as follows:
      1.   Before drilling below either of these casing strings, the blowout
prevention equipment shall include a minimum of three (3) remotely controlled
hydraulically operated blowout preventers with a working pressure which exceeds
the maximum anticipated surface pressure. The blowout preventers shall be one
annular type, one ram type equipped with blind rams and one ram type equipped
with pipe rams to fit the drill pipe being used. The BOP stack shall include a
drilling spool with side outlets if side outlets are not provided on the
blowout preventer body. A choke line and manifold and a separate kill line with
working pressure at least equal to three thousand (3,000) psi pressure shall be
installed ready for use. A fillup line shall also be installed ready for use,
and the fillup line shall be located above the annular BOP.
      2.   Two (2) blowout preventer tests shall be required, as follows:
         a.   Pressure Test:
            (1)   Ram type blowout preventers shall be tested with water to the
rated working pressure of the stack assembly. The pressure test required for
blowout preventers installed on surface casing shall be three thousand (3,000)
psi for the ram type blowout preventers and one thousand five hundred (1,500)
psi for the annular type.
            (2)   Pressure test shall be performed:
               (A)   When a BOP stack is installed;
               (B)   Before drilling out after each string of casing is set;
               (C)   Following repairs that require disconnecting a pressure
seal in the assembly; and
               (D)   Not less than once each forty five (45) days.
            (3)   All pressure tests shall be recorded in the driller's log;
and
         b.   Actuation Test: While drill pipe is in use, ram type blowout
preventers shall be actuated to test function once each trip. The annual type
preventer shall be actuated on the drill pipe once each week. All actuation of
the blowout preventers shall be recorded in the driller's log.
      3.   A blowout prevention drill shall be conducted each week for each
crew to ensure that all equipment is in working order to ensure that crews are
properly trained to carry out emergency duties. The oil and gas inspector may
require a blowout prevention drill at any time, well bore conditions
permitting, after conferring with the operator's representative. All BOP drills
shall be recorded in the driller's log.
      4.   The choke manifold shall contain as a minimum two (2) adjustable
chokes. The manifold shall contain provisions for bypassing the chokes and
diverting the flow away from the rig. The choke manifold shall be designed and
installed in a manner that will provide the least resistance to flow. Sharp
turns in the piping shall be avoided. Any sharp bends that cannot be avoided
shall be constructed utilizing targeted tees or block wells. The manifold shall
meet the recommendation of the latest version of API RP no. 53 for the class of
blowout preventer being used. The blowout preventer used in areas covered by
these rules shall meet or exceed the requirements for API class 3M. The piping,
valves, chokes, and all fittings, etc., located in the choke manifold shall
have a working pressure at least equal to the maximum anticipated casing
pressure. The choke manifold, choke line, and kill line shall be pressure
tested at the same time as the ram blowout preventers. The choke manifold and
choke line shall be anchored adequately to prevent movement during use.
      5.   The accumulator system shall be capable of storing sufficient
pressured hydraulic fluid to actuate the blowout preventers several times
without using the pumps. In addition, the closing unit shall be equipped with
pumps capable of closing the annular BOP in two (2) minutes or less without
using the fluid stored in the accumulator bottles. The functions of all BOP
control stations shall be clearly marked.
      6.   Other equipment regulations are as follows:
         a.   An inside blowout preventer (back pressure valve) or an
essentially full opening drill string safety valve shall be maintained in the
open position on the rig floor at all times. Sufficient inside blowout
preventers or safety valves shall be maintained on the rig floor to fit all
pipe in the drill string. A kelly cock shall be installed below the swivel;
         b.   Safety valves, and kelly cock shall be pressure tested to the
working pressure rating by applying pressure to the bottom side of the device
before drilling operations are commenced;
         c.   Pressure indicators shall be provided on the rig floor to
indicate the stand pipe pressure. A pressure indicating device to indicate the
choke manifold pressure shall be installed on the choke manifold;
         d.   A degasser or other approved equipment for removing gas from
drilling mud shall be installed in a proper manner ready for use before
drilling a potentially producible oil or gas zone or, if approved by the oil
and gas inspector, it will be acceptable to present a contingency plan to have
a degasser installed in a short period of time if required; and
         e.   When production or intermediate casing is being run, a valve with
connections to fit the casing being run shall be readily available on the rig
floor.
      7.   Blowout preventers shall be installed prior to working over an old
well or testing new zones. If tubing is to be pulled from or installed in a
producing well, blowout preventers and related equipment shall be installed and
operative. (1994 Code § 5-919)
   E.   Drilling Muds:
      1.   Mud System:
         a.   As noted in section
10-8-6 of this chapter, the proposed mud program shall be submitted. Except for
emergencies, this program shall be followed. Only additions approved by the oil
and gas inspector shall be added to the mud system.
         b.   The use of mud and the testing and control of mud properties
shall be such as are necessary to maintain pressure control of the well at all
times. Quantities of mud or mud materials sufficient to ensure well control
shall be readily available for use at all times.
         c.   The mud pit will be equipped with an electric level adaptor or
similar alarm system approved by the oil and gas inspector when drilling and
testing below the surface casing.
         d.   Before starting out of the hole with drill pipe, the mud shall be
properly conditioned by circulating with the drill pipe near the bottom until
the annular volume is displaced and the properties of the return mud are within
the normal range.
         e.   When removing the drill pipe from the hole, the annulus shall be
filled with mud before the mud level drops one hundred feet (100') below the
flow line. As a minimum, the hole shall be filled every five (5) stands of
drill pipe and every stand of drill collars.
         f.   The number of stands of drill pipe and drill collars that may be
pulled before filling the hole shall be determined and posted on the rig floor.
The number of barrels of mud (and the number of pump strokes) required to fill
the hole corresponding to the determined number of stands of drill pipe and
drill collars shall also be posted.
         g.   Mud test equipment to measure viscosity and density shall be
available on the drilling rig at all times, and comprehensive mud tests must be
performed every twenty four (24) hours or more frequently as conditions
warrant. (1994 Code § 5-920)
      2.   Water For Muds: No freshwater supply wells shall be drilled to
provide water for making drilling muds. (1994 Code § 5-921)
      3.   Disposal Of Muds: The disposal of drilling mud shall be by
transporting the mud to a state licensed disposal site. The mud may not be
buried in an earthen pit, pumped down the well bore or down the annulus of a
well, nor spread on the surface of the ground. (1994 Code § 5-922)
   F.   Casing:
      1.   General Requirements: Prior to running or cementing each casing
string, the operator shall notify the oil and gas inspector of his intentions
to set and to cement casing. Such notice shall be given sufficiently in advance
of such operations so as to permit the oil and gas inspector to be present when
the casing is run and cemented if he so elects. All casing, except conductor
casing, shall be new or approved used casing which has been pressure tested and
drifted. All pipe used must meet standard American petroleum institute (API)
specifications. A copy of all logs or other information associated with the
selection of the surface casing setting point and a copy of all bond logs shall
be filed with the oil and gas inspector.
      2.   Conductor Casing: Conductor casing shall be set to a depth of forty
feet (40') or to bedrock, whichever is less. The conductor hole shall be
drilled with freshwater and native mud or air. No chemicals or foreign
substances are to be added to the drilling fluid. The conductor casing shall be
cemented to the surface. This can be done by circulating cement or by using the
grout method. After cementing the conductor casing, drilling shall not be
resumed until the cement has set for twenty four (24) hours.
      3.   Surface Casing: Surface casing requirements are as follows:
         a.   Surface casing shall be set at a minimum depth to penetrate three
hundred feet (300') below the base of the treatable water zones (estimated to
be at 400 feet to 450 feet on the Johnson #1 log - section 20 T14N R1W) as
defined by the Garber-Wellington association or at a greater depth if required
by the state corporation commission or the oil and gas inspector in order to
protect both freshwater bearing strata and to remove any possibility of
contaminating fluids or gases reaching the surface through vertical fracture
systems or along fault planes. Surface casing shall be constructed of steel
casing grade J-55 or K-55. In setting the surface casing, centralizers shall be
installed on the casing as follows: one in the middle of the shoe joint, one at
the top of the second joint of casing, and additional centralizers not more
than one hundred feet (100') apart above the second centralizer. Pipe movement
during cement displacement is required. The casing shall be suspended off
bottom while cement is placed and allowed to set. The cement shall be
circulated to the surface and allowed to set under pressure for at least
eighteen (18) hours or any longer time required for the cement to reach a
compressive strength of five hundred (500) psi before reentering the well bore.
The cement is considered to be under pressure if one or more float valves are
used and are shown to be holding or if other means of holding pressure is used;
         b.   Casing shall be cemented with a type of cement approved by the
oil and gas inspector or his agents. Fracturing of the surface formations is
prohibited. The weights of cements used and the maximum allowable surface
pressures will be limited by this requirement;
         c.   If cement is not circulated to the surface, the oil and gas
inspector shall be notified. The cement shall be permitted to set, and small
diameter pipe shall be run down the outside of the surface casing to the top of
the hardened cement. Cement shall be pumped down the small pipe to fill the
annulus to the surface;
            (1)   A cement bond log, obtained using a dual receiver tool
(displaying the VDLTM/CETTM shall be run from the casing shoe to the surface,
and an acceptable bond must be demonstrable. Prior to running the bond log,
sufficient advance notice must be given as to permit the oil and gas inspector
to be present. In any case, the bond log must be submitted to the oil and gas
inspector within twenty four (24) hours of being run. If an acceptable bond is
not obtained, the casing shall be recemented until an acceptable bond is
achieved and demonstrated with an additional bond log. A drill stem test or
"dry test" of all squeezed perforations will be required;
            (2)   The casing shall be pressure tested by applying a surface
pressure until the pressure at the show shall exceed the maximum hydrostatic
pressure expected at that point during subsequent operations. Except, that
eighty percent (80%) of the internal yield pressure of the casing shall not be
exceeded. This pressure shall be held for one hour. Whenever the pressure drops
ten percent (10%) within the hour, the casing will be deemed inadequate and
shall be repaired and retested until the requirements hereof are met;
         d.   After drilling out the cement shoe twenty (20) to fifty feet
(50') of previously undrilled formation, the strength of the formation and the
cement outside the casing shall be tested by applying pressure inside the newly
cemented casing until the pressure at the shoe exceeds the maximum hydrostatic
pressure expected at that point during subsequent drilling operations; and
         e.   After setting the surface casing, the operator shall certify by
affidavit that the well has been set according to good engineering practices.
The affidavit shall stipulate the number of sacks of cement, the class of
cement, blending materials, weight of cement in pounds per gallon, cement
displacement, pressure, final pumping pressure, a description of the returns to
the surface, the depth to the cement measured after the cement has set, and
whether check valves (float shoes, float collar) held the pressure.
Commencement and completion times of such operation shall be stipulated. The
affidavit shall be completed by a cementing service company.
      4.   Intermediate Casing: Intermediate casing shall be set when required
by well conditions. Well conditions considered shall include, but are not
necessarily limited to, abnormal pressures, subnormal pressures, porous zones,
and fracture mud weights. The setting depth of intermediate casing will be
determined by the strength of the weakest exposed formation below the surface
casing shoe. In setting the intermediate string, centralizers shall be placed
in the area of the hole where cement is required at intervals no greater than
sixty feet (60'). A sufficient quantity of cement shall be used to cover the
shallowest potentially producible hydrocarbon zone by five hundred feet (500')
to isolate all hydrocarbon zones and to isolate abnormal or subnormal pressure
intervals. Cement shall be allowed to set under pressure for eighteen (18)
hours before reentering the well bore. A bond log shall be run from the casing
shoe to the top of the interval where cement is required, and a bond shall be
cemented until an acceptable bond is achieved and demonstrated with a bond log.
The intermediate casing shall be pressure tested prior to drilling out the
shoe. The bond log shall be submitted to the oil and gas inspector within
twenty four (24) hours.
      5.   Production Casing: Production casing and all related equipment
items, such as the well head, valves, etc., shall have a working pressure
rating sufficiently in excess of the highest formation pressure encountered in
the well. This string of casing shall be installed before completing the well
for production. In setting the production string, centralizers shall be placed
in the area of the hole where cement is required at intervals not greater than
sixty feet (60'). The casing shall be cemented in a manner to isolate all
potentially producible hydrocarbon zones, and the annular space shall be filled
with cement at least five hundred feet (500') above the uppermost producible
hydrocarbon zone. When a liner is used as a production string, the seal between
the inner and the next larger casing string shall be tested by a fluid entry or
pressure test to determine whether a seal between the liner and the next larger
string has been achieved. The test results shall be recorded in the driller's
log. (1994 Code § 5-923)
   G.   Completion Of Well; Affidavit Required: Upon completion of the well,
the operator shall certify by affidavit that the well has been set according to
good engineering practices. The affidavit shall stipulate the number of sacks
of cement, the class of cement, blending materials, weight of cement in pounds
per gallon, cement displacement, pressure, final pumping pressure and whether
check valves (float shoes, float collar) held the pressure. Commencement and
completion times of such operation shall be stipulated. The affidavit shall be
completed by a cementing service company. (1994 Code § 5-924)
   H.   Installation Of Tubing:
      1.   Before commencing the installation or removal of tubing on each well
where tubing is to be installed or removed, an approved blowout preventer shall
be placed in or at the top of the Christmas tree assembly at some point above
the master gate on the casing or oil flow string. The blowout preventer may, at
the option of the operator, be removed at the completion of the work of
installing or removing the tubing. If and when the blowout preventer is
removed, an approved type of head or support capable of supporting the weight
of the string of tubing and tested to withstand a minimum test pressure of
three thousand (3,000) pounds per square inch shall be installed on the well.
      2.   Before tubing is installed or withdrawn, the well shall be killed by
pumping or flowing a fluid into it, or a fan or pump or other device shall be
place at the top of the well, or in the well connections. The choice of device
shall be left to the operator of the well and shall be subject to approval by
the oil and gas inspector. The fan, pump or other device shall be designed and
operated to effectively remove or dissipate any gas or gasoline vapor coming
from the well or accumulating about the top of the well. At all times while the
tubing is being installed or withdrawn, adequate pumps, proper connections and
a sufficient reserve supply of fluid shall be available which will permit the
pumping or flowing of sufficient fluid to kill the well. If such device is
placed at the top of the well or in the well connections and it effectively
removes or dissipates gas or gasoline vapors coming from the well or
accumulating about the top of the well, it shall be deemed in compliance with
the provisions of this subsection. (1994 Code § 5-925)
   I.   Motive Power: Motive power for all well pumping equipment shall be
electricity unless otherwise approved by the oil and gas inspector. (1994 Code
§ 5-932)
   J.   Waste Disposal: Every permittee under this chapter shall be responsible
for the safe disposal of saltwater or other deleterious substances which may be
brought to the surface of the earth and shall provide a plan for such disposal
as required in section
10-8-6 of this chapter. Such disposal shall not result in pollution of the
waters of the town and shall not result in any other environmental hazard and
shall incorporate the best available techniques and equipment. (1994 Code § 5-
933)
10-8-17: SAFETY PRECAUTIONS:
   A.   Generally: Persons drilling, operating or maintaining any well shall
use all necessary care and take all precautions which shall be reasonably
necessary under the circumstances to protect the public. The provisions of this
subsection shall be deemed to be the minimum requirements for the preservation
of the public health, safety and welfare, and compliance with the terms hereof
shall not be deemed to relieve any persons of any additional duty imposed by
law. (1994 Code § 5-914)
   B.   Fire Prevention: Adequate firefighting apparatus and supplies approved
by the town fire department shall be maintained on the drilling site at all
times during drilling operations. All machinery, equipment and installations on
all drilling sites within the town limits shall conform with national fire
codes published by the National Fire Protection Association and such
requirements as may be issued by the fire department. (1994 Code § 5-926)
10-8-18: SURFACE FACILITIES:
   A.   Maintenance Required:
      1.   Whenever necessary to protect the health, safety or welfare of the
public, the oil and gas inspector is hereby authorized to direct any permittee
or operator to locate, relocate, remove or replace any well surface facilities
located within a particular drilling unit.
      2.   All lease equipment shall be painted and maintained in a good state
of appearance and shall have posted in a prominent place a metal sign no less
than two feet (2') square in area upon which the following information shall be
conspicuously inscribed: permittee's name; lease name; location of the drill
site by reference to the United States geological survey; identifying number of
the permit issued by the town; and other data as required by the corporation
commission.
      3.   Lease roads shall be maintained in such a manner as to safely and
comfortably allow for ingress and egress of town or state personnel traveling
in a common passenger motor vehicle.
   B.   As Built Drawings: A set of as built drawings shall be submitted
showing the relative locations of all equipment, valves, vents, drips, and
lines on the site along with a description of the capacities and working
pressures of the equipment and materials used in the pipelines.
   C.   Fencing, Screening And Landscaping: Fencing, screening and landscaping
regulations are as follows:
      1.   Fence Requirements: Any person who owns, operates or maintains any
producing well shall have the obligation to enclose the well, together with its
surface facilities, by a sightproof fence sufficiently high (at least 7 feet
high and topped by 3 strands of barbed wire) and properly built to keep persons
and animals out of the enclosure. All gates thereto shall be kept locked when
authorized persons are not within the enclosure. A duplicate set of keys to the
lock shall be filed with the oil and gas inspector. The oil and gas inspector,
upon written application by a permittee, may designate a different type of
fence to be erected if he determines in a particular case that a sightproof
fence is not necessary to protect the public health, safety and welfare; and
      2.   Landscaping And Screening Requirements: Any person who completes any
producing well located within seven hundred feet (700') of any occupied or
unoccupied dwelling or where the site is exposed to areas of high traffic shall
also meet the following sightproof landscaping requirements:
         a.   Such persons shall install evergreen vegetation or combinations
of landscaped earth berms and evergreens around those sides within seven
hundred feet (700') of the dwellings or those sides exposed to high traffic.
The berms shall enclose all fences or lease equipment and facilities;
         b.   The vegetation or berm combinations shall be a minimum of six
feet (6') in height; and
         c.   The vegetation or berms shall be maintained by the town after
viability of the vegetation is established.
   D.   Storage Tanks And Separators: Storage tank and separator regulations
are as follows:
      1.   All equipment shall be constructed to API specifications and so
certified. All equipment will be properly designed to handle the fluid loads
and expected pressure;
      2.   Any tanks located within six hundred sixty feet (660') of any
existing residence shall be pressurized and equipped with a vapor recovery
system. Any future development within this radius will necessitate installation
of a vapor recovery system;
      3.   Crude oil storage tanks shall not be constructed, operated or used
except to the extent of two (2) steel tanks for oil storage, not exceeding
three hundred (300) barrels' capacity each and so constructed and maintained as
to be vaportight. Additional tankage may be approved by the oil and gas
inspector. All storage tanks and separators shall be equipped with liquid level
switches and related equipment necessary to automatically shut off associated
wells and pumping to prevent the overflow of such tanks and equipment. These
"high level" switches, floats, and associated valves shall be maintained in a
working order at all times. If these detectors are found to be inoperative, the
oil and gas inspector shall issue an order to cease operations as provided
under section
10-8-29 of this chapter, and penalties as provided under section
10-8-28 of this chapter shall be assessed;
      4.   A permittee may use, construct and operate a steel conventional
separator and such other steel tanks and appurtenances as are necessary for
treating oil, with each of such facilities to be so constructed and maintained
as to be vaportight. Each oil or gas separator shall be equipped with both a
regulation pressure relief safety valve and a busting head;
      5.   Any tanks, batteries, separators, heater treaters, etc., shall be
enclosed with earthen or other acceptable retaining walls with a storage
capacity of at least one and one-half (1 1/2) times the liquid capacity of the
tanks within the storage; and
      6.   No drains shall be placed in the dike wall. Any rainwater or other
substance present inside the dike area and outside the tanks shall be promptly
removed. (1994 Code § 5-928)
10-8-19: PIPELINES:
   The requirements of section
10-8-14 of this chapter apply to pipelines. (1994 Code § 5-929)
10-8-20: SWABBING WELLS:
   A.   Compliance With Provisions: No person shall begin the operation of
swabbing an oil or gas well without first complying with the provisions of this
chapter.
   B.   Lubricator Required; Specifications:
      1.   No well shall be swabbed before a device, commonly known as a
lubricator, has been placed on the flow casing above all outlets or flow line
of the well.
      2.   The lubricator shall be not less in diameter than the flow casing
and equipped with an adequate oil saver or stuffing box at the top.
      3.   The total inside length of the lubricator shall be not less than
five feet (5') more than the total length of swab and turn back on swab line.
      4.   The lubricator shall have a nipple not less than two inches (2") in
diameter nor more than four inches (4") in length, placed not more than six
inches (6") from top and, on flow lines, between gate valve on the flow lines
and flow casings. The nipple shall be provided with a gate valve not less than
two inches (2") in size.
      5.   All equipment and parts of the lubricator shall be of a type
designed and tested to withstand a pressure of three thousand (3,000) pounds
per square inch. All pressure tests shall be by the hydrostatic method.
   C.   Tie Down Of Lubricator:
      1. The lubricator shall be tied down from the top to the casing next in
size to flow casing with wire line of not less than five-eighths inch (5/8") in
diameter or by iron or steel rods not less than one and one-fourth inches (1 1/
4") in diameter.
      2.   The tie down shall be equipped with turnbuckles and adequate clamps.
      3.   Guy lines shall be provided from the top and bottom of the
lubricator to the derrick legs. The guy lines shall be not less than five-
eighths inch (5/8") in diameter and shall be tied into the derrick legs at the
same level as on the lubricator.
      4.   A small gate valve at the top of the lubricator shall be tied back
to it, using clamps of required size. (1994 Code § 5-930)
10-8-21: SALE OR FLARING OF GAS:
   All produced gas shall either be sold or flared with the flaring procedure
being approved by the state corporation commission, oil and gas inspector and
town fire chief. (1994 Code § 5-931; amd. 2004 Code)
10-8-22: ANNUAL INSPECTION FEE:
   An annual inspection fee is hereby levied upon each well operated or
maintained under a permit issued by the town. Such fee shall be in the amount
as set by the town board 1 , payable to the town on or before the annual
anniversary date of the issuance of any permit under this chapter. No permit
for any well shall be considered valid for any year for which the annual fee
has not been paid. Failure to pay any required permit fee within thirty (30)
days will result in cancellation of the permit. (1994 Code § 5-935)
 
Notes
1 See section
  2-2-8 of this code.
10-8-23: CHANGE IN OWNERSHIP, INTEREST OR OPERATOR:
   A.   Ownership Or Working Interest: Within thirty (30) days of a change in
working interest or ownership, the town shall be notified through the oil and
gas inspector.
   B.   Operator: If a change in operator is desired, thirty (30) days prior to
the date of conversion, the prospective permittee shall affirm the original
application with a signature backed by a power of attorney. Prior to the final
approval of the new permit, all required insurance policies must be filed with
the town clerk-treasurer. The reapplication fee shall be set by the town board
by motion or resolution 1 . (1994 Code § 5-934)
 
Notes
1 See section
  2-2-8 of this code.
10-8-24: TEMPORARY ABANDONED WELLS:
   If the Oklahoma corporation commission grants the temporary abandonment
status to a well, the town, through its oil and gas inspector, must review all
the abandoned plans. In any case, all fees, tests, and reports must continue as
though the well were in operation. Initial and financial statements as to the
operator's financial conditions shall be submitted annually. These statements
shall be signed by a disinterested third party. (1994 Code § 5-937)
10-8-25: RECORDS KEPT; CONFIDENTIALITY:
   Copies of all applications, notices, forms, records, logs (when released by
the corporation commission), change in operation forms and all similar
